## Architecture recovery domain and proof checkpoint

This checkpoint starts from main `7d652fea79fddce8fd407f5c18dc2645ad203cc5`, tree `77de8bccba79228208c405b8c43582c4f00154f4`.
The [independently accepted domain](https://github.com/TechSpokes/typescript-wsdl-client/issues/266#issuecomment-6111899292) is `RE01-derivation-B-v1`.
Shared local rules and a concrete finite-evidence checker implement this domain while retaining explicit incomplete local obligations and the universal #234 theorem.
The original #231 branch and head `460f5b8379c68ffef79917284e445b5ab046429e` remain preserved; deliberate source ports do not accept that draft's assessment orchestration.

### Selected finite-extension interpretation B

Let Γ be the immutable actual graph, D its actual endpoint and A its actual clause-1.5 ancestor.
Initial qualification is structural and records outstanding actual obligations without assuming D's or a dependent RE01 judgment.
Every actual legality obligation retains Γ as its context.

A concrete hypothetical witness supplies finite fresh components F, intermediate E and endpoint D′ in the available environment H = Γ ∪ F with one coherent D replacement.
Original identities, owners, incidence, typed operands and source contexts remain anchored in Γ; D′ retains D's original effective non-derivation properties, with an immediate restriction edge to E, and E extends original A.
Every hypothetical reference to D resolves coherently to D′; source contributions remain evidence rather than invented hypothetical XML syntax.
Effective content includes inherited composition and every effective original AU identity; merely retaining canonical local contributions is insufficient.
Raw type properties, raw particle construction and restriction-normalized comparison views have separate authenticated owners.

The maintainer selected B: finite fresh internal types, declarations, particles, AUs and groups, and finite fresh global identities and affiliations are permitted.
Fresh global role/QName identities must be disjoint from the entire original global symbol domain, including originals omitted from a represented candidate view.
Original affiliation facts remain anchored, and every introduced affiliation still owes type/final/block, construction, attribution and declaration checks.

Permission to introduce a helper is not a successful witness judgment.
No finite helper-count bound, complete witness family or universal negative follows from finiteness of each supplied candidate.
Raw extension construction remains separate from restriction-normalized comparison.

Complete candidates retain every original record and every fresh addition, including inherited additions in a nested candidate.
Global lookup, absence and enumeration use that complete role/QName domain; experimental structural subsets cannot receive a complete semantic receipt.
Complete declared reverse-affiliation traversal includes abstract, blocked and invalid members before effective substitutability filtering.
Required declaration, affiliation and type checks therefore cannot disappear through filtering.

The hypothetical obligation roots are E, D′ and all F, followed by every concrete predicate/reference dependency they consume.
Availability alone does not require unrelated original definitions to be revalidated in H.
All actual obligations remain in Γ; an actual affiliation result cannot discharge a consumed hypothetical relation after D changes.
Original source-level components such as notations remain explicit source-universe operands even when they have no canonical node ID.
Unrepresented fresh categories and unqualified negative lookups return unresolved.

### Alternative A and recorded project decision

Alternative A fixes the original global symbol and affiliation universe while allowing fresh internal components.
It treats layer1's established declarations as the hypothetical question's external environment; the scoped raw-all fresh-affiliate mapping differs from B because A cannot introduce that affiliate.
Search simplicity is not a justification for selecting A, and abstract component representation alone does not justify B.

The dated `cos-ct-extends.1.5` does not explicitly select either helper universe.
Its construction note supplies a recipe, `concepts-conformance` permits abstract representations, `layer1` fixes definitions within an assessment, and `no-identity` leaves other identities incompletely specified; none settles the cross-assessment existential universe.
The October 11 addendum authorizes reassessment and explicitly does not certify replacement semantics.

Independent review recommended B as an explicit project interpretation, not as a claim that the normative text mandates arbitrary fresh globals.
The maintainer selected B in response to the reviewed alternatives, expressly requiring explicit lookup domains and complete affected obligations.
That selection and the subsequent independent contract review resolve the helper universe, lookup domains, component correspondence and required dependency policy.
The research comparison defaults to the accepted derivation model; the historical schema-preserving selector remains an explicit stronger experiment.
Structural/local kernels retain their stated scope and cannot self-certify complete semantic acceptance.
No public profile, catalog format, legacy default or faithful activation gate changes.
The primary source is the pinned 2004 Structures XML with SHA-256 `e496af408b14853e6169ac7c1fca09d55a81bd73771758ad6be020960e1317ba`.

### Selected raw matching, implicit order and generated scalar properties

The maintainer [selected three additional interpretations](https://github.com/TechSpokes/typescript-wsdl-client/issues/266#issuecomment-6114343952) after independent review of their discriminating cases.
These choices refine the B-domain local predicates; they do not discharge the full witness theorem or change the public profile.

Where a raw restriction matches a derived AU to same-name base uses, at least one such base use must satisfy the applicable compatibility checks.
An unmatched derived AU still follows the prescribed base-wildcard allowance.
Required-base coverage, requiredness, wildcard and other obligations remain independent requirements.
This existential raw-property matching does not fabricate an inherited source role or replace AU01 C1's universal checks for actual source replacements.

An implicit substitution-group set satisfies an order-sensitive restriction rule when some enumeration of that set satisfies the prescribed mapping.
Every explicit list retains its original order; QName, NodeId and discovery order are not governing enumerations.
One implicit particle/head has one coherent enumeration throughout a root restriction query, including repeated references and flattened blocks; separate queries choose independently.
Flattening preserves explicit blocks around each implicit choice, so its members cannot cross neighboring explicit positions.
A complete finite mapping procedure or equivalent criterion must preserve the one request budget, and interrupted search cannot establish a negative.

Generated simple-content scalar policy GSC1 creates one distinct anonymous scalar per exact source restriction slot, including zero-facet restrictions.
Its name and own annotation are absent, its target namespace is the containing complex type's mapped namespace, and its own final set is empty independently of complex final and schema defaults.
Its base is the exact selected starting scalar; variety, primitive, list item and ordered union members are inherited from that scalar.
The shared facet owner retains every applicable restriction check and facet annotation; fundamental properties follow the completed datatype's rules.
Extension inherits the exact generated identity, while repeated references share the component belonging to their original restriction slot.
The private property view preserves source-construction and scalar prerequisites; raw scalars still require their explicit complete abstract properties.
No invented XML simpleType, namespace declaration, public graph role or persisted representation supplies these facts.
Internal semantic references make the original slot available wherever a scalar reference is required, with separate context-qualified construction receipts and dependency closure.
Slot membership is independent of the containing complex type's membership, but suppressed source syntax does not create a slot and fresh source labels cannot create one.
An authentic original facet binding preserves original component properties without transferring their legality into a changed hypothetical context.

These are selected project interpretations, not retroactive claims that the dated primary text uniquely determines the choices.
The [authorized scalar continuation](https://github.com/TechSpokes/typescript-wsdl-client/issues/232#issuecomment-6141810918) selects P1 expression construction and evidence-qualified F2 restriction. General scalar containment and missing annotation evidence retain explicit implementation prerequisites; their selected meaning no longer requires a maintainer choice.
The [complete dated XML import inventory](content-model-s06-re-01-resolver-implementation.md#concrete-source-verification-apis) is now recovered from primary evidence and reviewed separately from these project choices.

### Conditional finite-evidence soundness

Let J(c,s) denote an exact obligation for authenticated context c and subject s in an independently accepted domain.
Consider a finite acyclic evidence graph whose edges are all nested obligations demanded by concrete local checkers, rather than a caller-selected subset.
Every node checks its complete local premises and exact child subject/context correspondence; missing children, duplicate storage IDs, malformed references and active backedges cannot establish success.

If every invoked local inference rule is sound and its premise inventory complete, acceptance implies the root J.
Finite acyclicity provides a topological order: primitive leaves hold by their established rules, and each later node follows from its checked local premises and earlier valid child judgments.
Induction reaches the root; exact context binding prevents substituting a judgment about another model or candidate.

Shared completed children are admissible only for the same exact obligation and context.
Resource interruption produces no accepted root, and legal recursive component references are not proof-dependency backedges.
The concrete [finite verifier](../src/compiler/finiteRe01Verification.ts) implements this induction over supplied evidence using privately authenticated context, correspondence and component receipts.
It emits `checked-candidate` only after actual obligations and every generated hypothetical prerequisite are discharged; retained unresolved local cases cannot pass.
The [independent controls](../test/unit/re01-candidate-independent.test.ts) include original typed AUs, a complete candidate, genuine nested helper evidence, missing child evidence, changed scalar properties, proof cycles and authentication boundaries.
The [generated-scalar controls](../test/unit/schema-original-generated-binding-independent.test.ts) add an actual extension D of a restriction R whose anonymous scalar must survive the hypothetical rebase.
The supplied E extends the designated `anySimpleType` ancestor, and D′ restricts E while retaining R's exact original scalar slot and AU properties.
The verifier records that slot's own actual and hypothetical construction evidence; a separately generated equal scalar cannot replace its identity.

Finite-evidence soundness does not establish evidence-family adequacy.
A successful arbitrary witness has not been proved to admit such finite acyclic evidence, and rejecting supplied cyclic evidence proves neither invalid XSD nor witness nonexistence.
Termination of checking a finite DAG also says nothing about termination of searching an unbounded family.

### Inherited fresh roots and the October 13 proof continuation

The [authorized continuation](https://github.com/TechSpokes/typescript-wsdl-client/issues/232#issuecomment-6141810918) retains context-local rechecking and the complete selected B domain.
The [new soundness countercontrol](https://github.com/TechSpokes/typescript-wsdl-client/issues/234#issuecomment-6141967632) starts from main `ffa4f3ea6d23fe628e70051c6bef5e6eb566ae62`, tree `c887431207d104f4c9f514d524dcb6d193a43c36`.
Its repair and the following lemmas require separate substantive review; they do not close #234.

#### Semantic success and the adequacy question

Let an obligation identify its target, designated ancestor, immutable actual environment, exact parent environment and selected domain.
Semantic success means that a permitted finite hypothetical environment supplies the two derivation steps, preserves the required effective properties and satisfies every applicable dated component predicate in its declared context.
This definition uses the component predicates and their existential requirements, independently of `FiniteRe01Evidence`, storage IDs or a `checked-candidate` result.

Actual-schema obligations remain separate judgments in the immutable actual environment, and a complete result must discharge them too.
Neither the target's nor another pending RE01 result is an initial qualification premise.
Legal component-reference recursion is distinct from the contextual recursion introduced when another existential construction is required.

The question remains whether every semantically successful permitted construction admits finite well-founded evidence using justified primitive leaves.
The primitive-root example below exposes a gap in the old root syntax; the other false positive concerns the checker's missing premise inventory, and the successful padding control concerns a proposed rank.
Repairing these concrete gaps does not establish universal adequacy for arbitrary nonprimitive constructions.

#### Primitive-root inadequacy and its repair

The [independently stated primitive example](https://github.com/TechSpokes/typescript-wsdl-client/issues/234#issuecomment-6142292378) uses empty `A` with immediate base `anyType` and `D` directly extending `A`.
`D`'s content contains an optional reference to global `M:D`, affiliated with original `H:A`, whose final set contains `restriction`.
The [source fixture](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/recovery/finite-proof-primitive-affiliation.xsd) has legal recursive component references and no other nonprimitive extension obligation.

The accepted primitive identity lemma proves semantic success without consulting evidence syntax: the first step is actual `D` extending `A`, and the second step is identity.
No restriction edge is introduced, so actual `M`'s affiliation reaches `A` using extension alone and satisfies `H.final`.
The [executable control](../test/research/re01/primitive-root-evidence.test.ts) checks all actual source and local component premises separately.

Every nontrivial candidate instead introduces the first edge `D' restriction E`.
Preserved endpoint content consumes original `M`, whose affiliation to `H` then forbids that edge under `e-props-correct.4`; additional helpers cannot remove the first edge or alter original `H.final`.
Thus this semantically successful primitive judgment admits no root of the old `FiniteRe01Evidence` format, which required a candidate context and a nontrivial endpoint restriction.

This is a counterexample to using that old root format for all RE01 judgments, not to the narrower contract of checking supplied nontrivial candidates.
The existing internal primitive leaves were sound but unavailable as the entry point's root.
It does not show that a successful nontrivial construction requires infinite nested evidence.

The verifier now also accepts `root: {kind: 'primitive-no-change'}` with a finite `nodes` array for other actual obligations.
It uses the same complete actual closure, exact primitive identity check, scalar and notation checks, typed-dependency discharge and evidence authentication as construction roots.
The separate `checked-primitive` receipt certifies the primitive target and all actual obligations; it does not claim a supplied candidate exists.

The contrasting extra-`U` fixture requires evidence for an unrelated nonprimitive original extension even when the requested root is primitive.
Invalid original scalar operands and an ancestor prohibiting extension still fail, a nonprimitive target cannot use the primitive marker, and publication obeys an inclusive sticky request budget.
Existing string-root input and `checked-candidate` receipts retain their contract; no public or persisted format changes.

#### A missing inherited obligation and its complete contextual negative

The [permanent control](../test/research/re01/inherited-helper-obligation.test.ts) uses original `B` extending builtin `string` and original `D` extending `B`, with the original typed attribute use retained.
The root construction changes `D` to a restriction of fresh `E` extending `anySimpleType`, and adds fresh `F` extending `B`, fresh head `H:B` and fresh member `M:F` affiliated with `H`.
The local affiliation succeeds in this parent environment because `F` derives from `B`.

The supplied nested construction changes `F` to a restriction of fresh `E2` extending `anySimpleType`.
The following identities and methods give a contradiction independent of candidate enumeration.

| Premise | Consequence |
|---|---|
| `F`'s designated ancestor is `anySimpleType` | Every intermediate extends that exact builtin directly |
| Original `B` extends `string` | `B` cannot be that intermediate |
| Fresh identities cannot equal original `B` | Renaming or copying `B` cannot put its identity on the new path |
| Proposed ancestry is `F -> intermediate -> anySimpleType -> anyType` | `F` cannot derive from original `B` |
| Fresh `M:F` and `H:B` remain required roots | `e-props-correct.4` fails in every permitted nested construction |

Extra particles, attributes, types, globals, affiliations or flags cannot change those fixed base identities.
This proves nonexistence for the contextual obligation of `F` in that parent environment over the full permitted helper domain.
It does not prove nonexistence for original `D`, which may have another root witness without these helpers.

At the starting revision, direct `checkComponentConstruction(child, M)` rejected `e-props-correct.4`, while the aggregate finite verifier returned `checked-candidate` for the same supplied evidence.
`CorrespondenceReceipt.fresh` correctly described only additions relative to the immediate parent, but the verifier incorrectly used it as the complete fresh-root inventory.
Inherited fresh `M` was available but absent from the child's checked closure.

The repaired verifier derives each hypothetical root inventory from all additions relative to immutable actual Gamma, plus its endpoint and intermediate.
It checks every inherited fresh component again in the exact child context, including declarations not reached from the new endpoint.
This restores the complete-premise condition required by the finite-evidence induction; it introduces no cross-context semantic transfer.

#### A successful construction that increases the proposed rank

Changing the fresh head's type from `B` to builtin `anySimpleType` removes the affiliation obstruction.
Both the original helper ancestry and the rebased helper ancestry reach that exact head type; all final and block sets are empty.
The contrasting complete two-node certificate succeeds and records `M` and inherited `E` in the child's own checked closure.

A third control adds two fresh string-content types, `G1` and `G2`, extending original complex `B`, while rebasing `F`.
The parent has one nonprimitive extension obligation, `F`; the child has two, `G1` and `G2`.
Thus the number of nonprimitive extension types increases on a successful permitted step.

Each `Gi` has an explicit witness: extend `anySimpleType` directly and restrict that intermediate to the same original builtin `string` content and attribute-use set.
Simple-base extension supplies the first step without another clause-1.5 obligation, and builtin `string` restricts `anySimpleType` with no new scalar facet.
The original integer attribute operand and use identity remain unchanged, and `M`'s head remains `anySimpleType` throughout.

Proving `G1` first requires a separate child proof for `G2`; proving `G2` first requires a separate child proof for `G1`.
Each branch eliminates one of those extensions and adds only a simple-base extension, so it reaches primitive leaves after two steps.
The complete control has six distinct candidate contexts and succeeds through the shared semantic owners.

This is a concrete failure of the unconditional extension-count rank, not a failure of finite-evidence adequacy.
The added types are dispensable in this example, so it also does not refute a helper-elimination theorem.
It shows why that theorem must justify selection of different witnesses rather than claim a decrease for every permitted successful step.

#### Conditional constructive extraction under a hereditary helper premise

Let `n(C)` count nonprimitive complex extensions in a finite context `C`, including inherited additions.
Assume every semantically true obligation encountered has a successful witness adding no new nonprimitive extension type, and that this property holds again for its required child obligations.
All new component categories remain permitted by this premise; its substantive restriction is on the existence of suitable witnesses, not on the admitted domain.

Replacing a nonprimitive target by a restriction removes exactly one count contribution.
Its intermediate directly extends the designated ancestor and is primitive, or has a simple base and no clause-1.5 obligation.
The designated ancestor and all other existing immediate bases are unchanged, so each remaining existing type retains its designated ancestor and primitive status.

Consequently each required child obligation occurs at a context with rank `n(C) - 1`.
Induction on this rank extracts finite evidence, once all local predicates and primitive leaves are justified in their own contexts.
The construction never identifies obligations solely by component ID and never assumes an active target valid.

For one obligation at rank `n >= 1`, a proof tree needs at most `P(n)` nodes with `P(1) = 1` and `P(n) <= 1 + (n - 1)P(n - 1)`.
Proving all at most `n` obligations in the initial context separately therefore needs fewer than `3 * n!` evidence nodes; no sharing between different contexts is assumed.
This bounds evidence branching under the stated hereditary premise, not the size of each finite witness or the work required to find one.

The general helper premise is now falsified by the [anchored support counterexample](#an-anchored-construction-requires-a-new-nonprimitive-extension).
The conditional extraction argument and branching bound remain valid under their assumption, but cannot discharge the complete B-domain claim.
The existing [fresh-affiliation and helper-type controls](#fresh-affiliation-controls-and-failed-reductions) rule out eliminating all new globals or replacing every helper type by an endpoint type.
Deleting a helper also changes complete substitution enumeration and can change restriction dispatch, ordering and attribution, so source identity alone cannot justify that deletion.

#### Fixed-point and direct-criterion alternatives

A finite fixed point over a supplied set of exact contextual obligations can decide that supplied system only after its local inference rules and fixed-point interpretation are justified.
Exact nested candidates have strictly increasing parent-context depth; merging repeated component IDs would discard their different environments.
The inherited-`M` countercontrol shows that the same component and dependency names can have different semantic answers across that merge.

A finite quotient would need to preserve component identities, complete lookup and affiliation observations, raw construction, scalar contexts, restriction mapping and all local failure conditions, together with witness extraction and reconstruction.
No finite quotient or stabilization bound over every permitted helper extension has been established here.
Selecting a greatest fixed point or accepting an SCC by itself therefore supplies neither adequacy nor mathematical search termination.

The fixed-ancestry contradiction above is an equivalent direct negative criterion for one contextual class and terminates without bounding helper count.
It does not cover the other combinations of AU, particle, scalar, identity and attribution constraints.
Fair enumeration, helper caps and exhausted accounting remain insufficient for unrestricted negative answers.

#### Revised remaining obligation and execution map

The continuation repairs a concrete sound-positive defect and a primitive-root representation gap, proves a contextual negative independent of finite search, and supplies a semantically successful countercontrol to an unconditional rank.
No semantically successful nonprimitive construction without finite nested evidence has been established, and no full-domain adequacy theorem has been established either.
This checkpoint proposed hereditary elimination of new nonprimitive extensions; the subsequent anchored support counterexample falsifies that premise.
A complete extraction or equivalent criterion must permit required new nonprimitive helpers and justify its recursive evidence discipline.

Even discharging that premise would leave witness-size or equivalent total-search reasoning for general negatives and mathematical termination.
Local authority and representation qualifications also remain premises; these controls use builtin types and original integer operands. The separately implemented P1/F2 scalar policies retain their documented general-containment and metadata qualifications.
#234 owns those proof obligations, with #232 joint acceptance and #179/#153 activation retaining their existing gates.

### Independent helper-reduction investigation

The October 13 standalone continuation starts from main `ad936e02b5a41029a8be8590845dd61c99fec401`, tree `3f0cc238cf42095af3e4ede751b983e916fdacb0`.
All seven recovery packages are accepted, including #268's complete local authorities from PR #286.
Accepted AU C1, PW R-240, DT A, GSC1 and P1/F2 remain premises; source/property qualifications remain explicit, and no local implementation gap supplies the missing universal theorem.

The quantified goal is a total existential decision over `RE01-derivation-B-v1`, including every permitted finite helper construction and its context-specific obligations.
Finite positive evidence, hereditary witness selection, witness-size coverage, complete negatives and mathematical search termination are separate claims.
This continuation establishes the following scoped contradiction and transformation counterexample, while leaving the universal claims open.

#### Installing a helper witness does not preserve parent predicates

The [source control](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/recovery/finite-proof-parent-observation.xsd) has empty A and its empty restriction R, original H:string, and D extending R with `sequence(H?, local c:string?)`.
The parent construction uses E extending A with `##any/skip` at `0..unbounded`, and D′ restricting E with its exact original particle.
The independently specified PW R-240 comparison accepts the complete formal `0..2` range, and the two original particle positions initially have different QNames.

An unrelated fresh F extends original S, whose base is builtin string.
A successful child rebases F to a restriction of E2 extending anySimpleType and adds fresh global c:string affiliated with H:string.
F retains the same string content; the affiliation passes by type identity, and all fresh child roots are checked in that child context.

Both finite proof steps succeed in their declared contexts, but D fails `cos-nonambig` when independently checked in the child: H's expansion now includes c and overlaps D's other optional c position.
The contrast without that fresh affiliate keeps D valid in both contexts.
The [executable pair](../test/research/re01/parent-observation.test.ts) therefore refutes installing arbitrary child witnesses into a parent without preserving its predicates; it does not refute the existence of different hereditary witnesses or expose a finite-verifier defect.

The child proof is a judgment about F in its exact parent environment, not a replacement legality judgment for the earlier D construction.
Inherited fresh-root checking cannot transfer an original endpoint's predicates into every descendant context.
A proposed simultaneous normalization must preserve or recheck those predicates and justify the resulting coherent witness.

#### A terminating contextual ancestry contradiction

Let X be a nonprimitive complex extension in a complete parent environment C with designated ancestor A.
Suppose an inherited fresh global M has type exactly X and immediately affiliates with H whose type is a distinct complex B.
M's freshness is relative to immutable actual Γ, so domain B makes it a mandatory legality root in every proposed child, including when M is unused, abstract or blocked from effective substitution.

Assume B is absent from A's unchanged ancestry and B's unchanged immediate edge is not extension to exact A.
Every permitted nontrivial construction forces `X restriction E extension A` followed by A's fixed ancestry.
The dated `cos-ct-derived-ok` identity, immediate-base and recursive-chain cases can reach complex B only if B is X, E, or on that ancestry.

B differs from X and is not on the fixed ancestry by premise.
An existing B cannot be E because its immediate edge is incompatible; a fresh E cannot acquire B's reserved component identity.
Simple-union alternatives cannot reach the required complex B, so `e-props-correct.4` fails in every such construction regardless of added helpers.

This proves nonexistence only for X's contextual two-step obligation in C.
It does not establish actual-schema legality, parent-candidate validity, or nonexistence for an original root that may choose another parent witness.
Primitive no-change, B identical to X, B on A's ancestry, and B itself eligible as E remain explicit exceptions.

[`proveRe01FreshAffiliationObstruction`](../src/compiler/re01CandidateCorrespondence.ts) implements this sufficient criterion through authenticated contexts and the existing ancestor, identity, declared-type and type-relation owners.
It returns `proved-no-construction` with exact context/target, criterion and offending affiliate/head, or `not-established` when the criterion does not apply; the latter is neither a positive nor a negative answer.
Missing properties or incomplete universes return named qualifications, and shared exhaustion remains `resource-limit`.

The procedure scans a finite parent universe and uses terminating local relations on its finite acyclic base graph.
It does not enumerate arbitrary helper extensions, so its negative proof has no helper cap or experimental cutoff premise.
All accesses and publication use the existing inclusive, precharged, sticky request budget; this finite criterion supplies no general search-termination claim.

Blanket helper deletion still fails the raw-all affiliation control; replacing helper types with endpoint types fails restriction blocking; arbitrary child installation fails the new UPA contrast; unconditional rank descent fails successful padding.
A finite fixed point would need a complete observation quotient with extraction and reconstruction, not component-ID deduplication or accepting an active SCC.
Fair enumeration remains only a positive semidecision, and the conditional evidence branching bound still leaves witness sizes and general negative termination unbounded.

The full gate therefore remains unmet after independent scrutiny.
The next proof action is hereditary witness selection with every obligation checked in its required context, together with an input-derived witness bound, or an equivalent total criterion over the same accepted B domain.
Preservation of earlier parent observations is required only when a chosen transformation installs or transfers facts across contexts; it is not a further requirement of every evidence-adequacy strategy.
#179/#153 activation and #184 payload ownership retain their existing boundaries; no profile restriction, policy change or production activation follows from this scoped result.

### Fresh complex types at the named-particle comparison

The [reviewed resumption packet](https://github.com/TechSpokes/typescript-wsdl-client/issues/234#issuecomment-6153288351) starts from main `f5451a55590b4a79da87ff6f176c0c160acc460e`, tree `71a7f9afd359d826adcc14db0a2a6b7df3377d0b`.
This continuation establishes a necessary type condition and investigates remaining helper roles without assuming child installation or narrowing the accepted domain.
All seven recovery contracts and selected P1/F2 remain premises.

#### Exact necessary condition and proof

Let C be a closed, resolved parent context and H its authenticated direct candidate with checked endpoint correspondence.
Only X's existing immediate base changes, to `X restriction E extension A`, where A belongs to C; every other existing semantic type base remains the same component identity in H.
Require qualified finite acyclic ancestry, including valid simple bases and union-member roles; authentication and correspondence alone do not establish component validity.

Let T be a complex definition already in C and U a complex definition newly added in H.
If the actual [NameAndTypeOK](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#rcase-NameAndTypeOK) type call succeeds for T derived from U with extension/list/union excluded, then U is E and E is new.
Moreover, T's path reaches X through restriction edges only; if E already belongs to C, no new complex U can satisfy this call.

Before visiting X, T's base path contains only C identities.
After visiting X, it contains E, A and A's unchanged ancestors; a path missing X contains no new complex identity.
The complex relation's identity, immediate-base and recursive cases therefore reach a new complex U only at E, and each preceding nonidentity edge must be restriction.

The comparison stops on reaching E, so E's extension to A is not traversed by this restriction-only call.
When the path instead reaches a simple type, valid simple ancestry and simple-union alternatives cannot lead to the required complex U.
Complex simple-content definitions remain complex: the comparison reads their complex base identities, rather than substituting their scalar content identity.

This proves necessity, not success of every comparison to E or legality of a candidate.
An extension-descendant of X still fails this call; all other NameAndTypeOK conjuncts, construction premises and nested obligations remain required.
The result applies to an invoked type call, not every successful particle comparison.

#### Exceptions and shared-owner controls

Exact type identity and an existing intermediate fall outside the new-U premise.
A simple union target can admit a type through union membership without lying on its base path, so the complex-target premise cannot be dropped.
Both-global NameAndTypeOK and particle/original-particle identity shortcuts can succeed without invoking this type comparison.

The [source fixture](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/recovery/fresh-complex-name-type.xsd) and [strict controls](../test/research/re01/fresh-complex-name-type.test.ts) use authenticated correspondence and the existing [particle](../src/compiler/particleRestriction.ts) and [type](../src/compiler/schemaTypeRelations.ts) owners.
Distinct local declarations have the same p QName, 0..1 bounds, empty flags and no values or identity constraints, isolating the type conjunct.
The controls cover X and a restriction-descendant reaching fresh E, a distinct fresh U, a path missing X, a forbidden extension prefix, existing E, exact identity, complex simple content, a simple-union exception, and particle identity.

Missing helper properties remain `unresolved`, and an explicit test budget produces sticky `resource-limit`; neither supplies a false type relation or existential negative.
These local relation receipts retain their component-validity debts and do not certify the fixture's arbitrary helper additions as a complete witness.
Closed membership, literal correspondence and qualified ancestry exclude unresolved references first binding to a new helper, rewrites of other existing bases, and invalid simple-to-complex base or member constructions.

#### Remaining observable helper roles

| Role | Observation and owner | Coverage of the named-type lemma |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Intermediate construction | Raw prefix/suffix, all placement, original identity; `particleExtension.ts` | Does not choose raw particles |
| Named restriction mapping | QName, bounds, flags, fixed values and exact type; `particleRestriction.ts` | Only existing-complex to fresh-complex calls |
| Affiliation and fresh heads | Immediate type/final and complete head chains; `schemaTypeRelations.ts` | Different excluded methods |
| Effective membership | Methods, blocks, abstract flags, names, absence, set size and implicit order | Fresh-member self mappings remain uncovered |
| UPA and EDC | Full expansion alphabet and named-type identity; particle owners | Changed expansion must be checked where consumed |
| Simple content and AU | Typed fixed/default operands, scalar identity and inherited raw uses | Complex simple-content ancestry only |
| Recursive ownership | Sharing, containment, raw content dependencies and exact context | No evidence-adequacy result |
| Fresh legality roots | Every addition relative to immutable actual Gamma, including inherited roots | All checks retained by the finite verifier |

Attribute declaration types must be simple, so a complex helper cannot directly replace an AU scalar type.
Complex helpers can still carry simple content, particles, AU sets and recursively consumed dependencies that require their own validity.
Simple, union, affiliation, global, particle and primitive helpers remain permitted; no blanket deletion follows from this inventory.

Wildcard restriction mappings and fresh-to-fresh comparisons are not existing-complex T comparisons.
In particular, a fresh global added to a retained head's expansion can occur on both sides of a restriction comparison, with global or identity shortcuts.
The lemma therefore does not eliminate the helper types of those globals.

#### A complete helper witness distinguishes two-head membership

The [membership source](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/recovery/helper-membership-vector.xsd) has empty A, primitive P extending A, and HP and QP both typed P.
HP affiliates with QP; HP blocks restriction, QP blocks extension, and their final sets are empty.
A fresh U extending P and fresh M:U affiliated with HP make M effective under HP and excluded under QP.

The [shared-owner controls](../test/research/re01/helper-membership-vector.test.ts) verify a complete two-node construction for empty X extending R, where R restricts A.
Its root uses E extending A and X restricting E, with fresh U and M; U's child uses unchanged existing P as intermediate and U restricting P.
All inherited fresh roots are checked in the child, and both proof steps succeed in their own contexts.

In the child, M's immediate affiliation remains valid, but its membership reverses: HP excludes the new restriction, while QP permits it.
This is a successful context-local proof, not a transfer of the parent's expansion facts or a candidate-verifier defect.
The existing ancestry obstruction correctly returns `not-established` because P itself is an eligible intermediate.

Replacing M's type with existing P admits M under both heads.
A fresh primitive extension of A cannot reach exact P, while a restriction of P loses membership under HP.
Those contrasts are derived from the dated substitution rule and exercised through the existing relation owner.

More generally, retaining M's positive HP membership forces a type path to P without restriction.
No other existing type in this source has that path; a new proper extension-only descendant of P introduces a nonprimitive extension because P's base is A, not anyType.
Fresh affiliation bridges cannot avoid QP's ultimate type predicate because the retained HP-to-QP affiliation remains fixed.

Thus pointwise replacement by existing or primitive types while preserving this exact two-head membership vector fails, even for a complete finite witness.
This is a stronger requirement than hereditary witness selection: complete alternative X witnesses using existing P or a fresh restriction of P also pass with changed vectors.
The source's empty X does not require this vector, so the control does not establish that every successful X witness needs a new nonprimitive extension.

#### An apparent discriminator with an impossible child

The same source separately has B restricting A, H:B affiliated with Q:A, H blocking restriction and Q blocking extension.
A fresh U extending B again gives M effective under H and excluded under Q, but B is not an eligible intermediate for U.
The mandatory inherited fresh M forces the existing ancestry criterion to prove no construction for U in that exact parent context.

Local affiliation success in this second family therefore cannot be promoted to a successful complete-witness counterexample.
The finite checker reports missing nested evidence, while existing-B and restriction-B alternatives produce checked complete X witnesses with different vectors.
This control separates local discrimination, contextual nonexistence and actual root nonexistence.

#### An anchored construction requires a new nonprimitive extension

The [anchored source](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/recovery/anchored-helper-support.xsd) makes the two-head vector necessary to the required endpoint mapping.
A has an optional sequence of global H repeated exactly four times, local z:string and a local Q:B/H:B choice.
B extends A vacuously, R restricts A to empty, and X extends R with an optional sequence of a twice-repeated H:B/H:B group, local z:string and global Q.

Global H:B affiliates with global Q:B; H blocks restriction, Q blocks extension, and final sets are empty.
Both globals are nonabstract, and the endpoint's local H declarations carry the required restriction block.
The required z delimiter separates prefix H positions from the final H/Q alternatives, establishing UPA in the actual and hypothetical contexts; all repeated names have exact named type B for EDC.

The [complete shared-owner control](../test/research/re01/anchored-helper-support.test.ts) chooses existing B as the intermediate and adds U extending B and M:U affiliated with H.
M is effective under H and excluded under Q: the first endpoint sequence maps to H's virtual choice by [MapAndSum](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#rcase-MapAndSum), with two members repeated twice giving exact range 4..4.
Q's expansion retains only the original Q/H names, which map to the last fixed local choice.

U's child restricts unchanged existing B, preserving U's exact A content and checking every inherited fresh component, including M, in that child.
Both finite proof nodes pass; checking the earlier original X again in the child fails particle restriction because H/Q membership changes.
That X is outside the child's required closure, so no cross-context predicate transfer or child installation is used.

To exclude every witness without new nonprimitive extensions, first exhaust the permitted intermediate shapes.
Existing B is the only existing direct extension of designated ancestor A; every fresh intermediate extends A and must preserve its nonempty element-only raw content.
[Particle Valid (Extension)](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-particle-extend) permits only the same particle or a 1..1 sequence with a recursively property-identical A particle first.

A's optional 0..1 three-member prefix cannot flatten or disappear: H remains nonabstract, z is required, and the last local choice remains nonempty.
Any surviving normalized suffix leaves the fresh intermediate's outer sequence at 1..1, incompatible with X's fixed 0..1 outer range.
Erased suffixes and singleton unit wrappers reduce to A's normalized comparison shape; required empty choices that survive normalization retain the same range barrier.

Property-equal prefix copies retain global H's scope, QName, type, flags and affiliation, and the last local alternatives retain exact Q/H QNames and types.
Reserved original global identities prohibit a fresh global from adopting H or Q's QName.
Thus neither prefix copies, annotations, new wildcard suffixes nor an alternative anonymous intermediate avoid these two shape cases.

In the remaining A-shaped comparison, the first endpoint sequence cannot map to H as an element: Group-to-Element dispatch is not permitted.
Ordered [Recurse](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#rcase-Recurse) cannot skip H[4..4], which is formally nonemptiable, so the sequence cannot align with a later base position.
Consequently some new nonabstract member M must be effective under H, turning that particle into a virtual choice.

Any such M also effective under Q has a new QName outside the final fixed local Q/H alternatives, and the final choice mapping fails.
This argument uses the missing QName match, not an injectivity or cardinality assumption about [RecurseLax](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#rcase-RecurseLax).
Every successful witness therefore needs an additional H-effective member that is not Q-effective.

H membership requires an affiliation path through unchanged H-to-Q and a type derivation to exact B without restriction.
Type B itself is Q-effective by identity, regardless of fresh affiliation bridges or the member's own flags; none of A, R or X supplies another eligible existing type path.
Rebasing X through existing B introduces a restriction edge, which H blocks; rebasing through a fresh extension of A cannot reach B at all.

Qualified simple or union types cannot derive complex B.
Any new proper extension-only descendant of B contains a new nonprimitive complex extension because B's immediate base is A, rather than anyType; anonymous helpers obey the same argument.
Restrictions are excluded by H's block, while an abstract member is not effective under H.

Hence every successful construction for this X introduces a new nonprimitive extension.
This refutes the precise no-new-nonprimitive witness-selection premise already at its root, including its hereditary form.
The complete two-node certificate proves finite evidence for this example, not failure of evidence adequacy or general impossibility of a complete decision method.

The controls contrast absent membership, existing B and restriction B, primitive ancestry failure, and raw live versus zero-normalized suffixes.
The universal conclusion follows from the exhaustive shape/type argument and independent nonauthor scrutiny; the finite controls alone would not establish it.

#### Alternative extraction and precise remaining gate

The proposed path-preserving type compression covers the named-type seam but cannot preserve every effective-membership vector.
A quotient identifying helper types solely by scalar content, endpoint reachability or primitive status also loses the successful two-head observation.
Those vector-only failures leave alternative root witnesses, whereas the anchored counterexample refutes the complete no-new-nonprimitive selection premise.

Support-oriented deletion or retyping repairs the empty-endpoint example, but cannot remove all nonprimitive additions from the anchored example.
A viable extraction must retain required helpers, including extension-method membership signatures, and re-evaluate each consumed restriction, UPA, EDC, scalar/AU and fresh-root predicate in its required context.
It still needs a well-founded evidence discipline or a complete contextual fixed-point argument beyond unconditional extension-count descent.

Deletion still has the earlier anchored raw-all falsifier, and endpoint replacement still has restriction blocking as a falsifier.
An observation quotient is an alternative only if it proves extraction and reconstruction for the predicates its decision method actually uses; preserving every parent's observations is not a prerequisite of a method that transfers no facts.
The exact failed proposition is no-new-nonprimitive witness selection, not a requirement to preserve all parent observations.
An input-derived finite helper/signature family with simultaneous extraction and reconstruction, or an equivalent total criterion over the same domain, remains unproved.
The counterexample does not rule out those alternatives or require a new semantic-policy decision.

Full witness/evidence adequacy, an input-derived witness-size bound or equivalent total criterion, complete negative answers and mathematical search termination remain open.
The conditional evidence-tree bound does not bound witness size, and configurable accounting or a rejected candidate cannot establish nonexistence.
No executable general search or general-negative branch is justified by the necessary lemma and these controls.

The next concrete action is a support/signature abstraction that admits the forced U-to-B extension and proves extraction, reconstruction and a terminating evidence or fixed-point discipline for the predicates it actually uses.
Preserve all inherited fresh-root obligations; require transfer preservation only for facts that the resulting method actually reuses or installs.

| Exact vector defeats pointwise type compression | `helper-membership-vector.test.ts` | Empty X admits changed-vector alternatives |
| Eligible existing intermediate supports complete helper proof | Same complete two-node control | General finite-evidence adequacy |
| Ineligible head forbids the helper's contextual child | Existing fresh-affiliation criterion | Other root witnesses remain possible |
| Changed-vector alternatives pass | Same finite verifier | Simultaneous arbitrary reconstruction |

### Helper-permitting construction investigation

This continuation starts from fetched main `b00b3eca2dbd65ae1bc3d2392afbf4eec155d481`, tree `71489468d92a5f480f61a7a5a79ccc66f06e6e3f`.
It consumes the seven accepted recovery packages and the [PR #288 disposition](https://github.com/TechSpokes/typescript-wsdl-client/issues/234#issuecomment-6154967973).
The accepted domain remains `RE01-derivation-B-v1`, with AU C1, PW R-240, DT A, GSC1 and selected P1/F2 unchanged.

#### Quantified claim and candidate families

For every qualified finite immutable actual Γ and contextual obligation `(C, X, A)`, semantic success quantifies over every permitted finite fresh extension of C satisfying the two derivation steps and all required component predicates.
The endpoint retains its anchored effective properties, actual obligations stay in Γ, and hypothetical obligations start from the intermediate, endpoint and every fresh addition relative to Γ, then follow consumed dependencies.
Qualification never assumes a pending RE01 judgment; contexts identify obligations, rather than component IDs alone.

The complete theorem would extract a successful representative in a chosen family from every permitted successful construction, reconstruct a valid construction from every accepted representative, and justify adequate contextual recursive evidence.
An input-derived finite bound, well-founded decision method or equivalent terminating criterion must then justify general negative answers.
Positive soundness, family coverage, evidence adequacy, complete negatives and mathematical termination are distinct gates.

The flat helper family adds fresh U extending an eligible retained primitive B and a fresh member typed U, with explicit child evidence rebasing U to a restriction of unchanged B.
For this hypothesis, eligible means B is a locally legal direct extension of X's designated ancestor and can be the exact retained intermediate; it does not mean every extension of B restricts B.
Its flat member representatives use existing types, one extension edge or restriction-only paths, without a path containing both extension and restriction to B.
This is an investigated representative family, not a restriction of the accepted helper domain.

The repaired helper hypothesis additionally retains restriction wrappers of U, so V restricting U can supply a member typed V.
It also retains the earlier required fresh global, abstract affiliation-bridge, simple/primitive helper and intermediate roles; those roles cannot be silently deleted by calling this a complex-helper family.
The extension helpers may use an eligible primitive intermediate retained in C or constructed for the root, but that choice still owes complete local and child evidence.
There is no established arbitrary-success extraction into the repaired family and no helper-count or witness-size bound for it.

#### Four local method signatures

Suppose nonabstract M has affiliation paths to three heads H, Q1 and Q2 with the same exact complex type B.
H blocks neither derivation method, Q1 blocks extension, Q2 blocks restriction, and none blocks substitution.
Assume the invoked type and affiliation premises are qualified and M is effective under H.

The dated [Substitution Group OK (Transitive)](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec) compares M's type path to the common exact B.
The complex-type blocks on that path are common to all three queries; H-effectiveness already shows that they exclude no method used by the path.
Consequently Q1 excludes M exactly when that path uses extension, and Q2 excludes M exactly when it uses restriction.
Arbitrary fresh affiliation bridges, member flags and helper type blocks cannot change this conclusion while retaining H-effectiveness.

| Path methods to exact B | H effective | Q1 effective | Q2 effective |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| None; exact B identity | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Extension only | Yes | No | Yes |
| Restriction only | Yes | Yes | No |
| Extension and restriction | Yes | No | No |

With B's final set empty and empty fresh type blocks, the four vectors have local representatives B, a vacuous extension U of B, a vacuous restriction of B, and V restricting a vacuous extension U of B.
This covers the method observation under the stated common-type premises, independently of ancestry path length.
It does not establish equality of raw content, original identities, component legality or contextual proof obligations for arbitrary constructions.

#### The anchored three-head counterexample and repair

The [source fixture](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/recovery/helper-method-support.xsd) extends the existing anchored example with H:B affiliated with Q1:B, itself affiliated with Q2:B.
A's optional sequence contains H repeated exactly four times, required local z, a fixed local H/Q1 choice, and a fixed local H/Q1/Q2 choice.
B extends A vacuously, R restricts A to empty, and X extends R with an optional sequence of a twice-repeated local H/H group, z, global Q1 and global Q2.
The three heads have the blocks in the preceding lemma, empty final sets and nonabstract declarations.

The previous exhaustive raw-prefix argument applies unchanged to the additional final choice.
Existing B is the only retained eligible intermediate; every fresh intermediate preserves A's nonempty optional raw prefix, and a surviving suffix leaves a 1..1 outer sequence incompatible with X's 0..1 range.
An erased suffix leaves A's comparison shape, while fresh QName reservations preserve its global H and fixed local alternatives.

The nonemptiable first H[4..4] position cannot be skipped, and the derived sequence cannot map to an element without H's additional effective member.
Some fresh nonabstract M must therefore be H-effective.
If M is also Q1-effective or Q2-effective, its disjoint fresh QName has no match in the respective fixed local choice after z; the final restriction fails.
This uses complete QName mapping, without an additional injectivity assumption about RecurseLax.

The common-type lemma now forces both derivation methods on M's path to exact B.
None of the retained types A/B/R/X supplies that path after X's rebase; simple types cannot derive complex B.
Flat representatives cannot meet the root observation, irrespective of fresh names, bridges or type flags.
Thus universal flat-family coverage is false; a family already permitting restriction wrappers is not refuted.

One repair uses U extending B, V restricting U and M:V affiliated with H, with U and V retaining B's exact content and empty AU set.
The parent maps X's H/H group to H's virtual choice by MapAndSum at exact range 4..4; Q1/Q2 retain only their original names.
U's explicit child restricts unchanged B, and the child checks inherited U, V and M in that exact context.
The complete two-node evidence succeeds through the existing semantic owners and finite verifier.

The opposite orientation, U restricting B then V extending U, also has complete two-node evidence here, with V's child restricting B.
Both representatives realize the required parent vector; their child makes M Q1-effective and X's earlier mapping fails there.
X is an original endpoint outside the child's required closure, so that observation is not a failed child construction.
No parent observation is transferred or child witness installed in the parent by either proof.

These are constructive reconstructions for this example, not a theorem interchanging the two orientations in arbitrary contexts.
The [twelve shared-owner controls](../test/research/re01/helper-method-support.test.ts) cover all four vectors, both complete reconstructions, missing nested evidence, renamed helpers and the content/consumption contrasts below.
The [independent reference controls](../test/conformance/reference/re01-helper-method-support.test.ts) establish the source premises without compiler imports and exercise the live engine's declared local scope.
The engine does not implement the complete RE01 existential rule.

#### Eligible B does not certify U's child

A further supplied U extends the same B with a live disjoint optional wildcard suffix after A's optional prefix.
Raw extension and M:U's immediate affiliation both pass, but rebasing U to restriction of B preserves a wrapper whose optional B prefix cannot cover B's required first H position.
The wrapper's outer `1..1` range does fit B's `0..1`; the precise failed comparison is the nested optional sequence's MapAndSum `0..4` against H's required `4..4`, as audited below.
The local restriction owner rejects that child with `derivation-ok-restriction.5.4`.

This refutes a reconstruction rule that checks only B eligibility and method signatures, then assumes the U-to-B child.
It does not refute the repaired family's alternative-witness coverage: this supplied live-content helper is not shown necessary for a successful root, and its failed child proves no root nonexistence.
Content/AU/final obligations must be checked through their existing owners, with explicit matching child evidence.

#### A consumed-root contrast and scoped nonexistence theorem

The [recursive fixture](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/recovery/helper-method-consumed-root.xsd) changes only both z declarations' types from builtin string to exact original X.
Element/type recursion is legal component-reference recursion, and both mixed parent constructions still pass the local X check.
The same two-node certificates now reject X's restriction in the exact helper child, because mandatory inherited M consumes H/B/A and A's z consumes X.
Here X belongs to the child's required closure; the positive fixture's outside-closure exception no longer applies.

For this recursive source, a permitted successful root construction does not exist, even with arbitrary finite helpers.
The exhaustive anchored argument again forces some fresh M effective under H and excluded under Q1/Q2, so its qualified complex type path to exact B contains both methods.
Choose T, the nearest extension on that path before reaching B; the prefix from M's type to T contains only restriction edges.

T is fresh and nonprimitive in this source.
None of retained A/R or the rebased X supplies an extension path to B, while reaching B's own identity ends the relation before traversing B's extension to A.
Every type before B has designated ancestor exact A; T's mandatory fresh-root legality therefore demands its own contextual construction.

In any child for T, inherited M's affiliation must still reach exact B.
T's forced path is `T restriction E extension A`; since B is not on A's fixed ancestry, the only possible E is unchanged existing B, the sole retained direct extension of A.
A fresh E cannot acquire B's identity, and other new helpers cannot alter the unchanged prefix from M's type to T.
If a fresh bridge loses its immediate affiliation legality in this child, that already rejects the child.

With E equal to B, M's path to B now uses restriction only.
The parent's H-effectiveness forbids every restriction block on the unchanged common type path.
If T is M's type, T's own type block is outside the intermediate-type block set in both queries; otherwise its block is already common to both paths.
Thus any child retaining valid affiliations makes M Q1-effective, because Q1 blocks only extension.

Inherited M and its affiliation consume exact H/B/A, whose unchanged z:X dependency makes the earlier X a required child check.
That X still restricts its parent-selected intermediate and has the same anchored effective particle.
If a previously erased suffix becomes live, its 1..1 outer range fails; otherwise the added Q1-effective M has no QName match in the fixed local H/Q1 choice.
X's local restriction fails in every child for T, contradicting the supposed successful root construction.

This is a full-helper negative theorem for the stated recursive fixture, independent of evidence-storage syntax, bounded enumeration or helper-count assumptions.
It is not a general-negative API, a failure of general finite-evidence adequacy, or an impossibility theorem for a complete decision method.
The permanent controls assert the two supplied candidate failures; independent scrutiny of the exhaustive shape, ancestry and consumed-dependency argument supplies the universal scope.

#### Alternatives and the remaining proof obstacle

Restriction wrappers repair the flat family's decisive method failure while preserving the original anchored U-to-B path.
The four-signature local quotient is finite, but it omits the live-content reconstruction obligation, immediate affiliation-final/bridge duties, complete expansion names and multiplicities, implicit order, raw particle incidence and scalar/AU identities.
A signature match cannot publish aggregate legality or merge exact contextual obligations.

Support deletion and choosing the opposite method orientation may provide different successful witnesses, as both reconstructions demonstrate.
Neither supplies a simultaneous extraction for all required helpers and consumed predicates; raw-all, blocking, inherited-affiliate and rank controls still constrain that proof.
An exact identity-preserving copy of an arbitrary supplied U is stronger than alternative-witness coverage and is not required.

The consumed-root negative shows why a copied-B helper's child cannot be assumed independent of the root.
Support-only helpers and the named/intermediate roles may have different observations, as the fresh-complex NameAndTypeOK lemma suggests, but consumed references can reconnect those roles.
A future extraction may exploit that distinction only after preserving complete required closures and justifying both positive and negative contextual cases.

For a supplied no-addition child discipline, replacing a nonprimitive extension by restriction removes one extension and adds none.
That finite count can justify finite evidence when every required child actually admits such a locally checked continuation.
The general repaired-family hypothesis permits new support extensions in those children, so it does not establish this hereditary descent or a stabilization bound.
The old conditional `3 * n!` bound still has a false selection premise and supplies no general witness-size bound.

An observation fixed point remains a possible alternative only after extraction and reconstruction preserve every predicate the method consumes and justify the chosen fixed-point interpretation.
Complete source contexts grow along exact parent forks; a component-ID SCC or fair enumeration alone cannot decide general negatives.
No impossibility theorem for a complete decision method or counterexample to general finite-evidence adequacy follows from these failures.

The exact open proposition is that every successful B-domain obligation has a repaired-family representative with adequate well-founded contextual evidence, together with an input-derived finite representative bound or equivalent terminating negative criterion.
Independent scrutiny accepts the scoped method necessity and concrete reconstructions while withholding that general theorem.
The resumption condition is a simultaneous extraction/reconstruction theorem for the repaired family, including bridges, fresh-to-fresh comparisons, raw content/AUs and all inherited roots, or a justified complete alternative.
No new policy decision, helper cap or engineering-budget change resolves this proof obligation.
