### Role-sensitive extraction and simultaneous copied-content reconstruction

This continuation starts from PR #289 on fetched main `024e20325cded24324c773ed486714d33a9858d3`, tree `c16b805524a40885beebc0a35881b6ae786b7f7e`.
It establishes a fixed-context relation observation lemma and a simultaneous extraction/reconstruction theorem for the copied-B fixture template below.
The template is a scoped proof example, not an accepted restriction of B-v1 or a complete general helper family.

The full quantified claim remains: every successful permitted finite construction over a qualified immutable Γ must have a valid representative with adequate complete contextual evidence, followed by an input-derived bound or equivalent terminating general-negative criterion.
Initial qualification does not assume any pending RE01 judgment.
The [preceding falsifiers](#helper-permitting-construction-investigation) and all seven accepted recovery packages remain binding.

#### Roles and predicate observations

Support-only describes how a helper is consumed by a particular parent predicate.
It does not exempt that helper from its own component legality, RE01 evidence or fresh-root checking in any required child.
A single type can serve support, named comparison and intermediate roles in different queries; the required observations are the union of those actual roles.

| Observation | Predicate owner | Role-specific preservation duty |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Exact type endpoints and segment methods | `schemaTypeRelations.ts` | Split at every queried identity |
| Entered type blocks and head flags | `schemaTypeRelations.ts` | Preserve each queried segment and head |
| Immediate base, ancestor and final | `schemaConstructionAssessment.ts` | Named/intermediate construction duties |
| Complete affiliation paths and bridges | Relations and declaration owners | Preserve declared census before filtering |
| Expansion names and multiplicities | `particleSemanticView.ts` | Retain every consumed member declaration |
| Explicit order and coherent implicit choices | `particleRestriction.ts` | Preserve blocks and repeated-head choices |
| Positive and negative role/QName lookup | `semanticContext.ts` | Preserve queried complete symbol domain |
| Raw particle identity, order and incidence | Correspondence and raw extension owners | Preserve consumed construction, not only language |
| AU identity, requiredness, value and wildcard | `schemaAttributeUses.ts` | Preserve original operands and selected matching |
| Scalar identities, facets and namespaces | Scalar and generated-slot owners | Preserve exact owning contexts and sharing |
| Declaration flags and identity constraints | `schemaDeclarationPrimitives.ts` | Recheck every consumed declaration |
| Ownership and shared components | `re01CandidateCorrespondence.ts` | Preserve anchored incidence and actual identities |
| Complete contextual closure and fresh roots | `finiteRe01Verification.ts` | Recheck all inherited F and consumed dependencies |

For example, affiliation legality uses the immediate head's final set; transitive effective membership uses the queried head's blocking constraint and type-path blocks.
An abstract bridge can be absent from effective expansion while remaining essential to immediate affiliation and required legality.
Existing affiliation/abstract-bridge, raw-all, original-intermediate, scalar, inherited-root and primitive controls retain these duties; this continuation does not replace them with a type-path quotient.

#### Fixed-anchor relation extraction and replay

Fix one qualified finite acyclic all-complex base graph and a finite set of exact query endpoints, called anchors.
No query in this lemma crosses into simple, list, union or generated-scalar derivation alternatives.
Between consecutive anchors on a base path, record its reachability, the union M of edge methods and the union K of blocks on entered vertices: exclude the departure vertex and include the arrival vertex.

For distinct anchors d and b, Type Derivation OK is true exactly when b is reached and the path's M is disjoint from the supplied excluded-method set.
For equal identities, it succeeds before traversing any edge, regardless of excluded methods or outgoing blocks.
These statements follow by induction over the complex branch of the dated [Type Derivation OK](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-ct-derived-ok).

For distinct element declarations, qualified complex substitution additionally requires affiliation reachability, absence of substitution blocking and disjointness of M from the union of queried head blocking and K.
The [transitive substitution rule](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec) uses exactly that union; intermediate affiliation heads' element blocks do not replace the queried head's block.
Immediate affiliation legality separately applies Type Derivation OK with the immediate head's final set, while complete effective expansion also filters abstract declarations.

Consequently extracting segment labels and replaying these qualified relation queries preserves their Boolean results, provided exact anchor identity/order, full affiliation paths, declarations and flags are preserved.
Concatenating segments unions M and K, with each interior anchor entered once.
This is simultaneous observation preservation for the specified relation queries, not reconstruction of legal component definitions or discharge of their receipt obligations.

The two mixed orientations have equal M to common B but different M to interior U.
For U extending B and V restricting U, distinct local equal-QName particles typed V and U pass the restriction-only type conjunct of [NameAndTypeOK](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#rcase-NameAndTypeOK); for U restricting B and V extending U, they fail it.
The global-declaration and original-particle identity shortcuts are excluded in these controls.

Similarly, V's own block is not entered on V-to-B, while U's block is.
Both paths can pass Type Derivation OK without exclusions while only one satisfies effective substitution under an entered block.
The [new live reference fixture](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/recovery/helper-role-anchors.xsd) accepts both leaves under their common B head and distinguishes them under exact interior heads blocking extension.

An anchor skeleton therefore repairs the loss of interior type observations in a common-B four-vector quotient.
It supplies no immediate-edge final, raw construction, scalar/AU, global-lookup or complete contextual evidence theorem.
Its anchor count and labels are derived from a supplied graph and query inventory, not from Γ alone.

#### Copied-B forest theorem and its exact scope

Fix Γ to [helper-method-support.xsd](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/recovery/helper-method-support.xsd), with both delimiter declarations typed builtin string.
Keep unchanged B as X's root intermediate and make X restrict B with its anchored effective properties.
Consider any finite fresh acyclic forest of complex types whose base paths terminate at exact B; each fresh type has B's exact original element-only particle, empty AU set, absent attribute wildcard and empty final/block sets, and is nonabstract.

Each edge is extension or restriction.
The only other additions are distinct fresh global nonabstract element declarations with empty flags, absent value/identity constraints and direct affiliation to original H; each is typed by B or a forest vertex.
Shared paths and unused fresh types are allowed, and every fresh type remains a legality root even if no member uses it.

No affiliation bridges, additional raw particles, AUs, scalar helpers or other additions occur in this template.
They remain permitted and potentially necessary in the general B-v1 problem.
No interior helper identity is consumed by a named restriction comparison, immediate-head final constraint or other additional role in this template.

For every locally successful parent in this template, per-member extraction into the repaired copied-B representatives preserves the parent's consumed observations and reconstructs complete finite contextual evidence.
The theorem quantifies arbitrary finite forest size and path length, rather than a tested helper cap.
It neither extracts arbitrary B-v1 witnesses into this template nor proves general finite-evidence adequacy.

#### Complete consumed closure for copied content

In every template context, a helper's copied particle consumes A's first global H position, the required string delimiter and the later fixed local H/Q1 and H/Q1/Q2 alternatives.
H consumes B and its fixed Q1-to-Q2 affiliation chain; complete incoming traversal can consume every fresh member, including members subsequently blocked under Q1 or Q2.
Those members consume their forest type paths, which all terminate at B, then A and builtin `anyType`.

The later local declarations are typed B, and no edge in this consumed universe refers to original X.
Copied-content correspondence introduces the same exact original particle/declaration/type operands; it adds no X dependency or generated scalar slot.
Complete fresh-root inventory and all receipt dependencies thus close inside this universe, including unused fresh types, while Γ still checks every original component separately.

Fresh member QNames are disjoint from all original names and from one another.
Only the first H[4..4] position expands them in the copied B particle; the required z delimiter separates it from later fixed local choices.
They create no new same-QName EDC pair, and the exact repetition/delimiter structure preserves UPA for any subset effective under a head.

No child transfers its earlier parent's X mapping.
Changing the delimiter to z:X breaks precisely this closure lemma, as the [recursive contrast](#a-consumed-root-contrast-and-scoped-nonexistence-theorem) proves.
The finite verifier controls explicitly check every inherited fresh root and the absence of X in the positive children's checked component closures.

#### Simultaneous no-addition reconstruction and evidence

All fresh local derivation steps pass: identical raw particle identity supplies vacuous extension or restriction; AU/wildcard and immediate-final premises are empty.
Each member's affiliation passes because its type reaches exact B and H's final set is empty.
The closure lemma supplies the remaining content, declaration and identity premises in every required context.

Let n be the number of fresh extension edges in the current template context.
For any required nonprimitive extension T, choose unchanged B as its intermediate and construct the child by changing only T's method/base to restriction of B, preserving its exact copied effective properties and every inherited addition.
B remains the primitive direct extension of designated A, and T's final restriction passes by particle identity and the empty other premises.

Every other inherited ext/rest step still compares identical copied content and has an empty immediate final.
All fresh members still reach B, and changed effective membership is checked afresh in that child's complete consumed universe.
The child introduces no type or declaration and removes exactly one fresh extension, so its remaining required nonprimitive extensions have rank n minus one.

At n equal to zero, the helper universe requires no further nonprimitive RE01 evidence; unchanged B has its accepted primitive no-change discharge.
Induction on n constructs complete acyclic evidence for every helper obligation in its exact context, retaining every inherited root.
For multiple obligations, supply each child's own descendant evidence; a proof from a sibling context cannot discharge it.

An unfolded certificate has at most the sum, for i from zero through n, of `n! / (n-i)!` nodes including its root.
This finite bound is in the supplied forest's n, with complete closure work at every node.
It is not a Γ-derived witness-size bound, and the old general `3 * n!` argument still has its falsified selection premise.
The induction establishes mathematical finite evidence with sufficient resources; arbitrarily large supplied forests may exhaust the unchanged configurable engineering limits.
Exhaustion remains a resource-limit result and cannot reject the theorem or establish nonexistence.

#### Per-member extraction without merging names

For each member, extract its edge-method union to B and reconstruct B itself, a vacuous restriction, a vacuous extension, or a restriction of a vacuous extension according to the four signatures.
Retain that member's exact QName, declaration flags and H affiliation; use disjoint fresh type identities for its selected support path.
Unobserved sharing between support paths may be split because no predicate in this template tests an interior support identity or distinct members' type identity against one another.

All H/Q1/Q2 expansions retain their complete declaration names and cardinalities, rather than one member per signature.
The anchored X particle compares original matching declarations; a fresh name has no local match in either later fixed choice.
Thus any locally successful parent requires fresh effective members to have both methods, and the repaired representatives preserve its restriction mappings and coherent implicit enumeration choices.

The reconstructed helpers satisfy the preceding local and no-addition evidence proof simultaneously.
Unused old supports can be omitted from the alternative witness; every support actually reconstructed is checked as a fresh root.
This extraction builds a new root candidate relative to immutable Γ, not an in-place fork that rewrites inherited non-target types or deletes inherited additions.

The opposite mixed orientation also reconstructs in this template, but its interior comparison differs as shown above.
The theorem therefore permits a chosen orientation only under its explicit absence-of-interior-role premise.
It supplies no rule interchanging orientations in an arbitrary context.

##### One replacement recipe and its admissibility

Let z be the exact original local delimiter declaration in B's first sequence, with fixed builtin `string` type, no affiliation, nonabstractness and unchanged source properties.
For every o in O, retain its complete node record except set `occurs = {min: '1', max: '1'}` and `term = {kind: 'element', declaration: local(z.id)}`.
Keep every helper/member record except the stipulated simultaneous exact-B helper content changes, retain X, and build the alternative root directly over Γ with precisely the original supplied membership.

This is one explicit recipe, not particle normalization or a generic transformation framework.
No new declaration, particle, helper or other component ID is derived; existing distinct IDs remain distinct, so no hash-injectivity or fresh-allocation existence premise arises.
All ID/owner/path/role derivations, owner availability and acyclic owner chains remain exactly the admitted ones.

#### General alternatives and unresolved proposition

The anchor-segment lemma supplies an adequate relation observation map; the copied-B theorem supplies one coherent content/AU/closure reconstruction and an actual well-founded evidence discipline.
Extending it to arbitrary successful witnesses still requires constructing raw content, scalar/AU properties, flags, bridges and named/intermediate roles simultaneously, with all consumed contexts and inherited fresh roots preserved.
The live-content child failure and recursive consumed-root negative expose the two concrete premises missing from that extension.

Exact copying retains arbitrary supplied helper complexity and offers no Γ-derived bound.
Per-segment signatures alone lose immediate-edge finals, named identities and raw incidence; merging members loses expansion cardinality and order constraints.
Support deletion cannot omit necessary global declarations or abstract bridges, and an arbitrary no-addition child need not pass restriction to B.

A fixed-point alternative must define its inductive or other justified interpretation, prove that contextual observations stabilize in an input-derived finite domain, and reconstruct valid complete witnesses from accepted states.
Growing parent forks with inherited additions are not component-ID SCCs; legal reference recursion alone supplies neither cyclic proof acceptance nor a complete negative interpretation.
Fair enumeration, a bounded constraint encoding and configurable exhaustion still supply no terminating general negative.

The precise remaining proposition is arbitrary-success simultaneous repaired-family extraction/reconstruction and general contextual evidence adequacy, followed by an input-derived finite bound or equivalent terminating complete criterion.
These scoped results do not refute that proposition or the existence of a complete method.
Beyond the exact-B bridge proved above, one concrete next action is to extend the anchor inventory to other immediate-head final and raw/AU/scalar roles, then construct a non-copied-content helper together with its complete required child rather than assuming the copied-B closure lemma.

The [shared-owner controls](../test/research/re01/helper-method-support.test.ts) retain both mixed positives, live-content failure and recursive negative, and add interior anchors, entered blocks, simultaneous opposite paths, padding extraction, sharing, unused fresh roots and missing sibling-context evidence.
The [independent reference controls](../test/conformance/reference/re01-helper-method-support.test.ts) cover the source premises and interior-head engine contrast without compiler imports.
No semantic owner, source API, failure category, resource default or general-negative branch changes; `verifyFiniteRe01Evidence` still sets universalCoverage, completeNegativeAnswer and searchTermination to false.

### Reviewed primitive no-change proof

The [independently reviewed primitive branch](https://github.com/TechSpokes/typescript-wsdl-client/issues/234#issuecomment-6112070199) applies when X directly extends its designated ancestor A and A's base is qualified builtin `anyType`.
The first step is that same extension; the second is an ε identity/no-change comparison of the same authenticated X and its effective properties.
It creates no component, clone or derivation edge.
Every other local component, extension, content, AU and declaration premise must pass first.
A's final set must permit extension; X's restriction prohibition does not apply to a nonexistent restriction edge.

Ancestor traversal starts at X's immediate base.
The specified builtin `anyType` self-base permits A = `anyType` for a direct ur-type extension, without permitting user-component self-loops or selecting X itself.
Equal property text on distinct components, arbitrary multistep extension collapse, changed properties and active proof cycles do not satisfy this identity rule.
This follows the rule's “possibly vacuous” qualification and the identity-first derivation guard; it establishes a concrete leaf, not universal witness adequacy.

### Conditional adaptive-read transfer

For a deterministic terminating evaluator, consider its complete adaptive semantic read trace.
The trace includes component properties, raw/normalized views, original scalar/type/namespace operands, caches, positive and negative QName lookup, full ordered collection enumeration and reverse affiliations.
If the same qualified algorithm in a second context returns the same semantic value for each corresponding read, with context-sensitive identities and caches correctly rebound or recomputed, it follows the same path and result.

The proof is induction over evaluator steps: equal prior state and the same next observation produce equal next state; the original finite trace bounds the induction.
Unchanged component records or equal reachable ID sets do not establish these premises.
The executable [dependency observations](../test/research/re01/predicate-dependencies.ts) check declared observation boundaries and authentic context binding; they do not discover a complete trace or issue transfer certificates.

The [independent controls](../test/research/re01/predicate-dependencies.test.ts) cover property, collection, omitted-global, negative-lookup and model/context boundaries.
Existing raw-all affiliation, helper-type/block, recursive endpoint, original-intermediate, fresh-AU and raw-composition counterexamples remain discriminants against weaker transfer or reduction claims.
Neither these controls nor the conditional lemmas select the helper universe or prove arbitrary-witness extraction/reconstruction.

### Remaining proof and implementation obligations

#234 still owes witness/evidence coverage or an equivalent total criterion, sufficient dependency transfer where used, complete negative answers and mathematical termination.
Local owner qualifications remain explicit prerequisites of any invoked rule.
F1 remains unproved; bounded failures do not disprove it and do not prove nonexistence.
#271 now supplies aggregate construction, generated evidence inventories and meaningful fully checked candidates within concretely discharged cases.
Its complete local acceptance still requires disposition of every retained implementation or interpretation gap; a successful example does not close that inventory.

The [independent checkpoint review](https://github.com/TechSpokes/typescript-wsdl-client/issues/234#issuecomment-6111899531) accepts the stated conditional lemmas under the selected domain.
The [executable checkpoint handoff](https://github.com/TechSpokes/typescript-wsdl-client/issues/234#issuecomment-6111745855) records the exact observations and known falsifiers.
Recovery completion, #232 joint acceptance and #179/#153 activation remain separate and open.

## Primary rule and interpretation

The dated [XSD 1.0 second-edition Extension constraint](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-ct-extends), clause 1.5, states:

> It must in principle be possible to derive the complex type definition in two steps, the first an extension and the second a restriction (possibly vacuous), from that type definition among its ancestors whose {base type definition} is the ur-type definition.

Its explanatory note states:

> This requirement ensures that nothing removed by a restriction is subsequently added back by an extension.

The recommended interpretation applies the numbered existential component constraint. The explanatory claim and its recipe of collecting original extension suffixes cannot impose an additional prohibition when a legal two-step witness exists. Optional original string content, removed and then restored as string or token, has a vacuous first extension and a valid final restriction.

An intermediate witness is a hypothetical schema component, not necessarily an already declared type or the concatenation of the original extension suffixes. The final comparison preserves the final type's assessed component properties and original constraints; its derivation edge is hypothetical for this check. Requiring the original derivation edge to remain identical would make the two-step requirement meaningless for a longer chain.

The ancestor is the type on the actual immutable derivation chain whose base is `anyType`, rather than an arbitrary earlier type. Follow original resolved edges, honor each original derivation's legality, and reject forbidden base cycles in the existing S05 stage. A first extension must also honor that ancestor's `final` exclusion of extension; reordering must not erase it.

### Source authority and corrections

The complete [pinned structures source](https://github.com/jacoelho/xsd/blob/142f25ee187e17f041af87b6bfcfc82385254520/docs/spec/xml/structures.xml) identifies the 28 October 2004 Recommendation and matches SHA-256 `e496af408b14853e6169ac7c1fca09d55a81bd73771758ad6be020960e1317ba`. Deleted editorial text is excluded when reading final clauses. The [published second-edition errata](https://www.w3.org/2004/03/xmlschema-errata) do not supply a replacement RE01 rule or a completeness proof for witness construction.

[PW01](content-model-s06-pw-01.md) separately records WG issue 2232's proposed occurrence adjustment; it is not an adopted XSD 1.0 correction. The universal wildcards used below have exactly `0..unbounded` bounds, where the competing occurrence readings agree. Subsequent maintainer selections are AU C1 with universal checking for source replacements, PW B and DT A; the joint handoff pins their reviewed records.

Those local selections do not settle RE01's hypothetical witness equivalence or source-incidence theorem. A selected scalar relation or source-replacement predicate is parameterized below without reopening its local decision.

## Component mapping and payload languages

[Particle Valid Extension](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-particle-extend) permits the same particle or a sequence with outer bounds `1..1` whose first member recursively preserves all base particle properties except annotations. [Particle Valid Restriction](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-particle-restrict) defines component mappings and its own pointless-compositor normalization. It does not ask an independent language-inclusion algorithm to override those mappings.

[Recurse](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#rcase-Recurse) requires a complete order-preserving mapping and requires each unmapped base member to be formally emptiable. [Particle Emptiable](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-group-emptiable) includes a group whose formal effective total range has minimum zero. The [choice range](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-choice-range) gives an empty choice a zero range.

A required empty choice therefore has `schemaEmptiable=true`, `nullable=false` and no accepted particle sequence. It is not a pointless optional empty choice. These different predicates are already separate in accepted #178 and must remain separate in the reordered witness check.

The controls `re-dead-wildcard-intermediate.xsd` and `re-dead-wildcard-final-restriction.xsd` have explicitly limited primary scope: normative component mapping only. A formally valid restriction can have a nonempty accepted language while its dead intermediate has none. That exposes a defect in the explanatory subset claim; adding a language-inclusion check would change the contract under investigation.

## Independent contrasts

The [fixed research manifest](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/re01/expectations.json) keeps primary recommendations, historical primary scope and each historical reference engine's observations separate. Eight original fixtures are copied byte-for-byte from the pinned draft with source revision, original directory and per-file SHA-256. Three additional fixtures independently contrast normalization and hypothetical AU incidence.

| Contrast | Formal result or remaining condition | Historical XMLSchema 4.2.0 | Historical libxml2 2.14.6 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Optional particle string restored as string | Vacuous witness | Accept | Accept |
| Optional particle string restored as token | Vacuous witness | Accept | Accept |
| Optional particle string restored as int | Dead separator and wildcard witness | Accept | Accept |
| Optional attribute string restored as string | Vacuous witness | Reject | Accept |
| Optional attribute string restored as token | Vacuous witness | Reject | Accept |
| Optional attribute string restored as int | Violates original attribute type preservation | Reject | Accept |
| Dead wildcard intermediate | Component mapping only | Reject | Accept |
| Restriction of dead wildcard intermediate | Component mapping only | Reject | Accept |
| Empty ancestor and repeated final sequence | Universal wildcard with no separator | Accept | Accept |
| Original chain with surviving fixed 1 and added fixed 2 AUs | RE equality/source-incidence gate | Reject | Reject |
| Hypothetical source with the same two AU predicates inherited | RE equality/source-incidence gate | Reject | Reject |

The particle-int row's pinned historical primary outcome remains `unresolved`; the new recommendation is explicitly identified as conditional on adopting the numbered-rule reading. This record does not silently rewrite that historical manifest or turn agreement among engines into authority.

[The current scoped reference tests](../test/conformance/reference/s06-re01-contract.test.ts) verify all eleven original fixture hashes and obtain fresh primary observations from `libxml2-wasm` 0.7.2/libxml2 2.15.1. Exact seventeen-pair payload checks and original versus hypothetical AU source-node identities remain separate selected assertions; fresh full-schema secondary validation remains unqualified.

### Particle integer restoration

Let `A` contain optional local `a:xs:string`, let a restriction remove it, and let the final extension restore required `a:xs:int`. A vacuous intermediate cannot satisfy `NameAndTypeOK`, and an intermediate containing both named declarations fails element-declaration consistency.

Instead construct `E=sequence(A, choice(), any[0..unbounded, ##any, skip])`. The first member preserves `A` exactly; the separator remains required and has no realization; the wildcard introduces no second named `a` declaration. The final `a:xs:int` maps to the wildcard through `NSCompat`, while `A` and the required empty choice are formally emptiable unmapped base members.

The wildcard cannot be reached after the required empty choice, so it introduces no competing reachable attribution position. The intermediate preserves the original `A` attribution behavior; no new named declaration introduces an EDC conflict. This is precisely the component-mapping witness preserved by the two pinned controls, not a payload subset proof.

### Attribute integer restoration

Every valid intermediate extension retains an attribute declaration with the original QName and original simple type, by `cos-ct-extends` 1.2. Distinct declarations with that QName are forbidden by `ct-props-correct` 4. An added attribute wildcard does not make an existing matching declaration disappear from the restriction rule.

The final integer attribute cannot be validly derived from the retained original string type under [complex restriction](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#derivation-ok-restriction) 2.1.2. This negative proof uses type preservation and QName uniqueness, rather than exhausting a candidate list. String and token restrictions satisfy the corresponding original type requirement; their optional-use and fixed/default constraints must still be checked independently.

### Pointless-prefix contrast

[The new schema](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/re01/pointless-prefix-repeated-final.xsd) starts with empty `A`, extends to a repeating choice, restricts to exactly seventeen `a,b` pairs, and performs a vacuous final extension. It independently exercises `MapAndSum`, repeated composite bounds and the reordered ancestor selection.

Appending a required empty choice plus wildcard preserves an outer `1..1` sequence, which cannot absorb the final repeated root through the sequence occurrence rule. A prefix absent after certified source mapping permits a single `0..unbounded` wildcard without a separator when the original raw extension checks pass, so normalization yields that wildcard as the complete intermediate particle. Every final group can restrict it under the agreed universal-wildcard case. Restriction-only pointlessness is insufficient for this branch.

An empty accepted language is not enough to take this branch. A required empty choice remains a meaningful particle and must never be converted to an absent prefix merely because it has no realizations or no terminal positions.

## Proposed finite particle family

The following is a conditional particle lemma over certified component views. It is not yet the complete complex-type decision procedure required for #234 closure. Its preconditions identify exactly which obligations remain outside the executable probe.

The owning component checker supplies source-valid schema operands, restriction-normalized views, the formal emptiability of the actual corresponding normalized members, and a complete restriction predicate for each original pair used by the mapping. It separately certifies the generated intermediate's `final`, mixed-content parity, all-group placement, source mapping and other component constraints.

### Candidates

| Ancestor component view | Candidate family |
|---|---|
| Any existing particle | Vacuous extension followed by checked restriction of the original pair |
| Certified disappearing comparison prefix, with raw extension legality checked | One `##any/skip` wildcard with `0..unbounded` bounds |
| Meaningful prefix, legal nonvacuous extension | Original prefix, required empty choice, one universal wildcard per unmatched final direct member |

The meaningful-prefix candidate applies to a final normalized element or a normalized sequence whose root bounds are `1..1`. A final element uses `RecurseAsIfGroup`; a sequence uses `Recurse`. All members matched to the ancestor retain original identity and bounds; each unmatched tail member maps to its own wildcard.

One wildcard is insufficient for several remaining direct sequence members: the order-preserving mapping must send successive restricted members to successive base members. The construction therefore uses at most the number of final direct members, instead of expanding repetitions or guessing an arbitrary candidate-length cutoff.

An ancestor whose original raw particle is an all group cannot simply be embedded in the sequence candidate, including a singleton all deemed pointless for restriction. `cos-all-limited` still applies before approval; vacuous witnesses remain possible. The executable probe makes the nonvacuous extension predicate an explicit caller input instead of declaring all such types outside the approved profile.

### Mapping and termination

Use states `(i,j)` for the counts of final direct members and original prefix members already considered. Skip prefix member `j` only if it is formally emptiable; match final member `i` only if the owning component-restriction predicate proves it restricts prefix member `j`. Both transitions increase `j`, and matching also increases `i`.

Reaching the end of the prefix yields a witness with one wildcard for each remaining final member. Cache each state and its predecessor once; there are at most `(r+1)(b+1)` states and no fixed-point iteration across element type references. Legal recursive element/type edges remain atomic IDs; forbidden containment/group cycles belong to S05.

The direct restriction predicate terminates according to its owning contract. Given that contract, the finite mapping search terminates with a witness, a failed family or an unresolved predicate. The probe's failed family result is `unresolved`, never `invalid-schema`.

Here `r` is `restricted.length` and `b` is `prefix.length` in the [historical probe](../test/research/re01/witness-probe.ts).
Both arrays come from caller-supplied `Prepared` views, not directly from immutable Γ.
The input-derived envelope and the remaining reconstruction premise are audited below.

### Conditional completeness argument

For a meaningful prefix, `cos-particle-extend` fixes every nonvacuous intermediate's outer sequence and original first member. Pointless normalization preserves the remaining meaningful original prefix; any valid final sequence mapping first consumes a prefix of final direct members in that original prefix. Each remaining final member mapped into an arbitrary suffix can instead map to its own universal wildcard.

The required empty choice makes every added wildcard unreachable without adding a named declaration. Its formal emptiability permits skipping it during `Recurse`; each universal wildcard can absorb any restricted leaf or group under the agreed `0..unbounded` rule. Thus a valid ordered prefix mapping supplies a constructive witness independent of the original extension suffixes.

For a certified disappearing comparison prefix, no original meaningful particle remains before the suffix after restriction normalization. A single universal wildcard suffices after normalization and admits all finite or unbounded original repetitions in the group-to-wildcard rule. Source-mapped absence is one case; a real mixed-content empty sequence is another, provided its raw extension is independently legal as established in the audit below.

This argument is a useful finite particle construction, not a published or accepted full-domain theorem. Before using failed family search as a negative proof, independent review must establish that every source-valid normalization path, all placement and component identity case falls into these branches and that the full type's AU/scalar constraints can be synthesized consistently. The prototype intentionally refuses to infer that theorem from its positive tests.

### Input-derived meaningful-prefix capacity audit

This October 18 continuation establishes a structural capacity and conditional particle-mapping reconstruction, with outcome B for complete contextual reconstruction.
It starts from actual main `0194853a6fc683a9f3d980259f0e16d3c2ce83d8`, tree `fc3ba816262bf1a1f2154778d4e432ea0c261987`.
The preceding supplied-family theorems remain separate results with their original scopes.

#### Quantifiers and immutable source of the bound

Fix finite structurally prepared immutable Γ, its selected ancestor A and endpoint D, with element-only effective raw particles P and R mapped from their original source operands.
For each permitted successful hypothetical context H with those exact corresponding operands, consider only the meaningful-prefix branch in which H's restriction-normalized R is an element or a sequence with root bounds `1..1`.
This is a condition on H: fresh affiliations can change an actual element into a virtual choice, so an actual element does not put every hypothetical witness in this branch.

Compute W on Γ's effective declared particle DAG, expanding model-group uses through the existing view owner, before substitution expansion or restriction pointlessness.
A terminal or empty compositor has W = 1; a nonempty compositor has W equal to the sum of its children's W.
Sum each child incidence, including repeated references to the same shared child; memoizing its W does not remove repeated contributions.

Effective source composition wrappers are included.
Occurrence values and element/type references are atomic operands: neither a finite repetition nor component-reference recursion is unfolded.
Set BΓ = W(P) and RΓ = W(R); these finite integers are computed entirely from Γ, though they can exceed the number of distinct graph records.

In H, let r be the final direct-member count, using a singleton for an element, and b the meaningful prefix count, splitting only a normalized sequence at `1..1`.
Then r ≤ RΓ and b ≤ BΓ, hence the historical coordinate grid has at most `(RΓ + 1)(BΓ + 1)` states for one fixed qualified set of pair/order answers.
A successful prefix path consumes i final members, so its replacement tail has k = r − i ≤ RΓ wildcards.

The proof is structural induction over the immutable raw particle DAG with edge multiplicity.
Zero elimination and ordinary flattening remove or expose original structure already counted in W; singleton removal can expose an original sequence but introduces no new sequence members.
A virtual substitution expansion introduces a choice, never a sequence: multiple virtual alternatives remain inside one direct sequence position, while a singleton choice can expose only the original child's structure.

Thus arbitrary fresh affiliations cannot increase this direct sequence frontier beyond W, even though they can change child kinds, pair outcomes, formal summaries, branch membership and implicit order assignments.
The coarse capacity also counts retained required empty choices as one structural incidence; an empty language is not particle absence.
This argument bounds direct element/sequence frontiers only, not all normalized nodes or the alternatives inside virtual choices.

The small typed [capacity function](../test/research/re01/meaningful-prefix-bound.ts) reads authenticated actual effective declared views through the existing owners and returns exact decimal capacities.
It does not select the RE01 ancestor, certify actual legality, classify every H, enumerate orders or supply a candidate.
Its `outside-slice` marker for other content kinds describes this measurement's scope, not unsupported B-v1 input.

#### Extraction and particle reconstruction

For a successful nonidentity raw extension in H, `cos-particle-extend.2` fixes the outer `1..1` sequence and requires recursively equal first-particle properties, except annotations.
It does not require the first particle to have original P's identity: the current owner also checks distinct property-equal copies.
The extraction below covers the exact-original-prefix subfamily.
Unconditional transport of comparisons from a distinct property-equal prefix to original P is false under accepted B-v1 identity, as the [anonymous-type continuation](#copied-prefix-transport-and-optional-prefix-bypass) proves; general existential reconstruction remains open.
With a meaningful normalized prefix, normalization retains a nonempty leading segment consisting of P's normalized direct members, or P itself when it is not a unit sequence.
An ordered successful final-to-intermediate mapping therefore consumes an initial segment of final direct members in that prefix, skipping only prefix members whose formal emptiability has been established in H.

Retain those exact successful prefix comparisons and their coherent implicit-choice assignments.
Replace the remaining mapping by the particle expression `sequence1..1[P, choice1..1[], any0..unbounded/skip × k]`, sharing original raw P rather than flattening or copying it.
Each unmatched final direct member maps to a distinct successive wildcard; all skipped prefix members and the required empty choice are formally emptiable.

Every element or wildcard satisfies the universal namespace constraint, and `skip` is the weakest processing mode.
For a group member, accepted PW R-240 and #178's exact total ranges discharge the group-to-wildcard cardinality comparison against `0..unbounded`.
The original group bounds and members remain unchanged, including exact large finite values; no repetition expansion or conversion to a JavaScript number is used.

The required empty choice has no realization and shields the added wildcards from reachable attribution, while its formal schema emptiability permits the restriction mapping to skip it.
No named declarations are added by the tail, so EDC adds no declaration comparison; legal placement and UPA of the retained raw prefix remain necessary.
In this element-only non-all placement branch, the outer sequence preserves exact raw extension structure and the existing declared-view authorities check the resulting local construction.

The expression uses k + 2 new particle records: the outer sequence, the required empty-choice particle and k wildcard particles, with compositor terms represented inline by the canonical graph.
It shares every original prefix component and needs no fresh element declaration; a fresh intermediate type is one additional record.
This conditional allocation count is distinct from the grid size and from the number of records needed by other helpers or evidence.

The particle mapping implication assumes admitted identities for these records and qualified prefix comparisons in the reconstructed context.
It proves neither that a total fresh allocator exists nor that every old comparison survives changing H.
No hash-injectivity assumption or identity-representation amendment is used.

#### Audit of historical probe premises

The [probe controls](../test/research/re01/witness-probe.test.ts) test the supplied-view grid, original occurrence operands and explicit resource results.
They do not authenticate an actual/candidate context or construct the intermediate.
In particular, omitted `normalizationCertified` and `nonvacuousExtensionAllowed` flags are treated as allowed, `schemaEmptiable` is caller data, and `Restriction` can be an arbitrary answer table.

| Historical premise | Current semantic owner and remaining duty |
| --- | --- |
| Raw prefix and nonvacuous extension | `checkRawParticleExtension`; base-first property equality, not automatically original identity |
| Original effective source operands | `deriveActualComplexContent`; source composition retained separately from hypothetical raw properties |
| Hypothetical raw content | `deriveRawComplexContent`; explicit candidate properties, without source remapping |
| Restriction normalization | `prepareSemanticParticleView`; authenticated H, complete substitution census and original origins |
| Formal emptiability and exact ranges | Single #178 occurrence analysis attached to those semantic views |
| Original pair restriction | `checkParticleRestriction`; type/scalar prerequisites, exact operands and coherent implicit orders |

| Identity admission and references | Actual/candidate context and type-property validation; no freshness from unequal path strings |
| Full dependencies and helper children | `verifyFiniteRe01Evidence`; every fresh and inherited root in each required context |

These owners are the accepted [complete local authorities](content-model-s06-re-01-resolver-implementation.md#complete-268-rule-inventory), not a witness-existence oracle.
Their local receipts still carry separate component and contextual prerequisites.
The bound does not discharge any pending RE01 child by assuming the theorem under investigation.

A second complete control supplies E with two optional universal wildcards and retains fresh F restricting E with two required positions containing the same exact original `a:int` declaration.

#### Exact path and final condition

Let X be the set of initial helper extension vertices, excluding primitive E.
For any subset J of X, let C_J retain every component and property except change each x in J to restriction of exact E in both canonical and raw derivation.
Every child prescribed by the preceding induction has this form, and all subsets occur along its unfolded branches.

For p=q, immediate affiliation succeeds in every C_J by exact type identity, regardless of H.final.
The identity comparison stops before reading the type's outgoing edge, so rebasing the shared declared type does not disturb it.
For p≠q, the following three conditions are necessary and sufficient for M's mandatory immediate affiliation to succeed in every C_J.

1. Exact q occurs on the initial base path from p to E.
2. If q≠E, no vertex of X occurs on the segment [p,q), including p and excluding q.
3. H.final is disjoint from mu(P) union R(P), defined below for that exact segment P.

Here mu(P) is the union of initial edge methods on p→q, excluding q's outgoing edge.
R(P) is `{restriction}` when [p,q) contains an extension vertex, and is empty otherwise.
The criterion concerns exact type identities and paths; equal content or a common E ancestor cannot replace condition 1.

For reachability, rebasing an extension vertex strictly below an interior q changes its outgoing base to E and deletes q from p's path.
If no such vertex exists, rebasing q itself or any ancestor above q cannot affect the segment ending at q.
For q=E, every rebase still reaches E, including when p itself is rebased.

For q=E, in a changed path take the first rebased vertex x encountered from p.
Its new segment is the unchanged p→x prefix followed by one restriction edge to E; later rebases lie beyond the cut and do not affect that segment.
Thus every new method belongs to mu(P) union R(P), the unchanged context realizes every method in mu(P), and rebasing any extension vertex realizes the added restriction.

The union across all C_J is therefore exactly mu(P) union R(P), not merely a sufficient overapproximation.
The complex relation excludes H.final on every traversed edge and succeeds on the identity case; this proves condition 3 and the stated necessity/sufficiency for this stipulated rebase discipline.
It does not prove that every witness must use that discipline.

| Declared types | Permitted H.final for this discipline |
| --- | --- |
| p=q | Any admitted set |
| Strict p→q with q interior | Restriction-only segment; restriction absent from final |
| Strict p→E with no extension vertex | Restriction absent from final |
| Strict p→E with an extension vertex | Empty final |

An interior head may itself be an extension helper with ancestors that are extensions.
Those vertices lie outside [p,q); imposing restrictions on them would conflate a comparison's stopping identity with the type's own separate construction obligations.
All those construction obligations remain required and are discharged below.

### Research recognition of the three-position inherited consumer

This slice starts at actual main `6de486c672c7c4a792ddc3a186a2e18f9a0c2925`, tree `f8135275b6a03e8d7a2fd46c1af09d60a35da73f`.

#### Premise-to-owner map

| Decisive premise | Authority and recognition check |
| --- | --- |
| Complete immutable actual and inherited parent | Authenticated `SemanticContext` handles; same request and exact `originalContext()`; candidate parent; complete component/scalar/notation universes |
| Nonprimitive extension target and forced first restriction | `checkComponentConstruction` supplies local premises and a nonprimitive `re01` duty; `re01Ancestor`, `immediateTypeBase` and exact type identity verify its ancestor and immediate complex base |
| Distinct exact complex target/head types | `resolveSemanticType` and `declaredType`; head type is the target's immediate base, with element-only raw content; no type-content equality substitutes for identity |
| Retained declaration properties | Exact authenticated nonabstract global declarations; distinct identities/QNames; member's declared type is target and direct affiliation is head; empty head final and restriction in head block through `declarationMethods` |
| Mandatory nonabstract member | Member is nonabstract; effective-group same-declaration rule preserves its own leaf under arbitrary added affiliates |
| Inherited child-context consumer duty | Selected consumer and its restriction base are fresh relative to actual; raw/canonical restriction edges and content selectors agree; both remain inherited roots under B-v1 |
| Immutable raw operands | Exact distinct unit sequence roots/use particles, each with three raw children: shared prefix, required head/member use, shared required empty choice |
| Forced normalized middle mapping | `deriveRawComplexContent` and `prepareSemanticParticleView` verify three explicit outer positions: optional local element, required head/member block, required empty choice |
| No QName/identity/normalization/wildcard bypass | Prefix local QName differs from member; roots/uses distinct; no wildcard in either consumer; virtual leaves have no original-particle shortcut; canonical global uniqueness is enforced by context preparation |

#### Sufficient criterion and first-edge separation

Let the hints select target T, head h, member m and derived restriction consumer C in a complete authenticated inherited parent P over immutable actual Γ.
Require the map above, including C restricting a distinct retained consumer B, B restricting exact h.type, and normalized content `sequence(local[0..1], h-or-m[1..1], choice()[1..1])` with shared first/last raw operands.
The local prefix is a single element in the restriction view; its raw representation may be the exact unit sequence flattened by the existing owner.
Target and head type are named global complex definitions; all selected type raw records must agree with canonical selectors and have element-only content.
The consumer types are nonabstract, with empty AUs/attribute wildcards and empty type flags.
The raw prefix is original to Γ; all four distinct consumer root/use particles and both consumers are fresh relative to Γ.
No restriction is placed on the number of other admitted helpers or affiliates under either h or m.

The reusable first-edge argument is the [fixed-parent theorem's type argument](#reusable-first-edge-obstruction): nonprimitive T must restrict an intermediate in any permitted child.
Exact T differs from exact h.type, so any successful derivation to that complex type contains the first restriction; missing that type fails derivation outright.
Restriction blocking therefore removes m from effective(h), with no identity/simple-union escape and without assuming T's RE01 validity.
The recognizer checks this argument's premises separately from the consumer shape; it does not sample a child or search for an intermediate.

The retained nonabstract m remains in effective(m) under arbitrary permitted finite additions, including direct/transitive affiliates under m.
Every h alternative is global and cannot have m's reserved QName, including after arbitrary additions under h.
The immutable three-position outer sequences force the middle mapping; complete RecurseLax must map m to a base leaf of its QName and cannot do so in any coherent order.
The required empty choice survives normalization, the local prefix cannot accept m, distinct roots/uses prevent identity, and no consumer wildcard exists.
Thus C necessarily fails its inherited child-context restriction duty before nested evidence could help, proving no complete T child of this exact P.

This guarantee is contextual and sufficient only; unmatched shapes prove nothing, and immutable Γ is never classified as impossible.
Missing qualification, invalid input and sticky shared resource exhaustion remain distinct from `not-established` and a criterion-tagged proof.
The selected IDs are lookup hints only; no Boolean premise or caller-created receipt is accepted.
General search, other shapes, arbitrary witness coverage/bounds, copied-prefix transport and production activation remain outside this slice.

### Any nonempty finite flat prefix of optional local elements

This continuation starts at actual main `f87fe681f14ad4691634b7cd049fa15fdb9e4d20`, tree `70e55301563001961e16830ed7296ac31a53695f`.
Retain every preceding criterion premise except the single-element prefix shape.
The shared original prefix P is either one direct local element particle occurring `0..1`, or a unit sequence of n ≥ 1 direct local element-particle incidences, each occurring `0..1`.

The prefix sequence and every leaf particle are original to Γ, and each leaf references a scoped local declaration whose expanded QName differs from M's.
Count ordered child incidences, including repeated references to the same particle ID; a set of identities does not measure sequence length.
Pairwise distinct prefix QNames are unnecessary for this obstruction; independent parent/child legality still applies.

#### Normalization under arbitrary permitted finite additions

The exact inherited raw consumers remain `sequence1[P,H1,C]` and `sequence1[P,M1,C]` with their distinct roots/required uses and shared required empty choice C.
Under [cos-particle-restrict.2.2](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-particle-restrict), a unit sequence nested immediately in a sequence is pointless, so P contributes precisely its n leaf incidences; a direct P contributes one.
No optional local element is a pointless compositor, and the nonempty prefix has no empty group to remove.

Permitted additions cannot rewrite these original particles or attach substitution-group members to a local declaration: [e-props-correct.3](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#e-props-correct) requires global scope for affiliation.
The prefix leaves therefore retain their kinds, occurrence ranges, order and incidence count in every possible child, regardless of new type/declaration helpers.
If some addition or retained component is illegal, that child already fails; assuming its legality cannot introduce another prefix shape.

By cos-particle-restrict.2.1, the H/M use remains an element or becomes an implicit choice of element leaves, never a sequence.
A singleton choice can reduce to its surviving element without changing its one outer position, and C is a required empty choice, which 2.2 does not discard.
Thus both outer unit sequences retain exactly L = n + 2 ≥ 3 explicit positions in every permitted finite child; neither outer sequence is a singleton or empty compositor.

#### Complete ordered mapping and inherited contradiction

[Recurse](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#rcase-Recurse) requires a complete functional mapping from all derived particles, including those with minimum zero.
Its definition of order-preserving requires each image to follow the preceding image, so the image positions f(1), …, f(L) are strictly increasing integers in {1, …, L}.
For each i, the preceding i − 1 images imply f(i) ≥ i and the remaining L − i images imply f(i) ≤ i; hence f(i) = i.

Optionality can justify skipping an unmapped base position only when a complete derived mapping still exists; it never deletes a derived prefix incidence.
The required M position n + 1 must therefore map to the required H position n + 1, independently of prefix length, shared IDs or any optional prefix values.
The [separate first-edge argument](#sufficient-criterion-and-first-edge-separation) excludes M from effective(H), while nonabstract M remains a mandatory effective(M) leaf and global QName uniqueness excludes an impersonating target.

The preceding RecurseLax/NameAndTypeOK argument now applies unchanged at that position, for arbitrary direct/transitive affiliates under both H and M and every coherent implicit-choice order.
Neither distinct inherited roots/uses nor fresh virtual leaves acquire a same-particle shortcut, and neither consumer has a wildcard.
This proves no permitted complete target child of that exact parent, before any nested evidence could repair the inherited consumer duty.

For n = 1 it preserves the exact `inherited-three-position-consumer-v1` result representation; for n > 1 it publishes `inherited-flat-prefix-consumer-v1` with `prefixLength` equal to the incidence count.
These are sufficient criterion tags, not a general RE01 decision or a new semantic authority.

Removing restriction blocking yields complete successful certificates for n = 1, 2 and 5, including expanded H/M groups; growing blocked groups also preserve the obstruction at n = 2 and 5.
The [independent reference controls](../test/conformance/reference/re01-particle-continuation.test.ts) inspect the new original operands and complete ordered-mapping contrast without importing the recognizer or semantic owners; live primary observations qualify both new source schemas separately.

Empty, nested, choice, all, optional-group, required-element, repeated-element and wildcard prefixes remain unproved by this criterion.
Explicit successful exact-usage and one-below work/node controls preserve inclusive accounting and sticky exhaustion; resource failure, invalid input, missing qualification and unmatched shapes remain distinct.
The proof uses arbitrary finite n and additions; fixed test lengths support its discriminators and do not establish its quantifiers.

No production code, B-v1 domain, finite-verifier claims, budget default, accepted AU C1/PW R-240/DT A/#178/GSC1/P1/F2 policy or original operand contract changes.

### Copied-prefix transport and optional-prefix bypass

The [source fixture](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/re01/copied-prefix-anonymous-types.xsd) contains groups G1 and G2, each with an optional local `a` whose inline anonymous complex type is empty.
The two local declarations and their type definitions have distinct original identities/owners, while their corresponding properties compare equal under accepted B-v1.
Ancestor A uses G1, B restricts A to empty and endpoint D extends B with G2.

#### Original inline-element syntax qualification

This source exposed a concrete defect in [the original syntax owner](../src/compiler/schemaSyntaxAssessment.ts): the canonical builder correctly maps each inline type through its local ElementNode, but also retains that same syntax child on the element ParticleNode.
The generic retained-child rejection therefore rejected valid original complex/simple inline types before complete witness verification.
Canonical inventories and original source evidence remain unchanged by the repair.

The syntax owner now recognizes only a particle's local scoped declaration at the exact same source URI, digest, path and span, with a local anonymous simple/complex type owned by that declaration at a direct child source.
Only the single retained child of matching XSD kind and exact type source is delegated; the particle's child-order check includes that owned type, so annotation ordering remains enforced.
Child-component syntax and semantic validity remain separate obligations rather than being assumed from source correspondence.

The [source-owner controls](../test/unit/schema-syntax-assessment.test.ts) cover valid inline complex/simple types, retained inventories, stray/misplaced children, late/duplicate annotations, duplicate types, named/ref plus inline types and independently altered source/type/retained operands.
Malformed source forms retain construction or syntax rejection, and exact source/span/type ownership mismatches do not earn delegation.
The [independent source/reference control](../test/conformance/reference/re01-particle-continuation.test.ts) inspects both original owned inline definitions and records live primary acceptance of the source with absent/present optional `a` instances.

#### Strong comparison transport is false under accepted identity

Let P be A's original G1 particle and Q be D's original G2 particle.
The [complete typed control](../test/research/re01/copied-prefix-transport.test.ts) checks recursive component-property equality of the two local declarations, while type derivation from Q's anonymous type to P's exact anonymous type fails.
The local types are distinct restriction-of-anyType definitions, and property equality does not create a derivation edge to the other exact type.

A fresh primitive intermediate E with raw content `sequence1[Q,C]`, for required empty choice C, passes raw extension of A because Q and P have corresponding properties under cos-particle-extend.
D restricts this E through its exact Q operand, and the entire finite certificate passes with every actual and fresh component checked in its required context.
Replacing Q by exact original P in that otherwise unchanged E preserves raw extension but makes D's local-element NameAndTypeOK type duty fail; the complete verifier rejects this candidate at `derivation-ok-restriction.5.4` on D.

Thus property equality plus restriction into Q does not imply restriction into exact P under accepted B-v1.
This is a counterexample to that strong comparison-transfer premise, not a universal assertion about every implementation's treatment of anonymous XSD types or a proof that no alternative witness exists.
The source, property equality and restriction relation are independently qualified rather than assuming the failed source syntax premise.

#### Optional-prefix reconstruction survives this counterexample

Use exact original P in E and append C followed by a `##any`, `processContents=skip` wildcard with range `0..1`.
The optional original P can be skipped, C is formally emptiable, and D's optional Q leaf maps to the wildcard, so no comparison of the two anonymous declared types is needed.
The complete finite verifier accepts this replacement and the `0..unbounded` tail contrast, with the original declarations, anonymous owners and all unused roots retained.

The copied witness, failed no-tail recipe and successful original-prefix tail witnesses remain separate controls.
They refute strong transport while establishing a concrete alternative reconstruction; a failed recipe does not refute general witness existence or finite-evidence adequacy.
No cross-context receipt is transferred, and every predicate is recomputed in its owning context.

This establishes only the displayed optional-prefix case, not arbitrary prefix bypass, helper/evidence bounds, complete general negatives or mathematical termination.
General arbitrary-success coverage and contextual evidence adequacy remain open; the following investigation tests inherited consumed roots at every finite evidence leaf.
All accepted contracts, authority versions, failure categories, configurable sticky budgets and later activation gates remain unchanged.

### Renewing helper duties and the finite-evidence boundary

This investigation starts from committed `dabf3c5d5879c4d17ba258f0d502bd1054e7576e`, tree `be820215dbaefe85f3884f9519724c9cd611f93f`.
It distinguishes finite primitive-leaf evidence from an indefinitely locally productive family over the same accepted B-v1 domain.
Neither result supplies the missing equivalence with independent semantic success.

#### Exact source and locally qualified judgments

The [consumed source](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/recovery/helper-renewal-consumed.xsd) retains the [two-head anchored source](#an-anchored-construction-requires-a-new-nonprimitive-extension), changing both z declarations to exact original X and Q's block to both extension and restriction.
H and Q remain nonabstract globals of exact named complex type B, H affiliates with Q and blocks restriction, and their final sets remain empty.
A's optional outer sequence contains H[4..4], required z:X and the fixed local Q:B/H:B choice; X's optional outer sequence contains the twice-repeated local H:B/H:B group, required z:X and global Q.

B directly extends A vacuously, R restricts A to empty and original X extends R.
Complete original local/source checks pass; the sole nonprimitive actual obligation is X, while B has its existing direct primitive discharge.
X's actual existential judgment is the problem being investigated, never an assumed input qualification.

Here a locally qualified candidate means exact authenticated correspondence and all required source/local component, scalar, AU, declaration, attribution and construction premises, except the generated RE01 existential duties being studied.
Calling such a candidate locally qualified does not call it a complete witness.
All primitive discharges use the existing direct-only rule; a vacuous multistep U→B→A path is not a new primitive leaf.

#### A live nonprimitive duty in every required local closure

The earlier exhaustive anchored shape argument applies to any permitted root intermediate and continues to apply to retained X in any locally qualified descendant.
Raw extension requires the same A particle or a unit sequence beginning with a recursively property-equal copy.
A's optional nonempty prefix cannot disappear; a surviving suffix leaves an outer range `1..1`, incompatible with X's fixed `0..1`, while erased suffixes reduce to the A-shaped comparison.

In that comparison the first endpoint group cannot restrict an element, and complete ordered mapping cannot skip H[4..4].
Thus H needs another nonabstract effective member M to create its implicit choice; MapAndSum then retains the exact total range 2 × 2 = 4.
The final Q expansion can contain only original Q/H names, because every other global QName lacks a target in A's fixed local Q/H choice.

Consequently every qualified mapping needs a fresh-QName member effective under H and excluded under Q.
H blocks restriction, so M's qualified type path to exact complex B contains extension edges only; identity B would also be Q-effective, even through fresh affiliation bridges.
The path is therefore proper, and Q's two-method block excludes its extension-derived member.

Every type before B on this extension-only path is fresh relative to immutable Γ and nonprimitive: retained A/R cannot reach B, and X's already replaced restriction edge is H-blocked or misses B through a fresh intermediate.
Simple/list/union alternatives cannot derive exact complex B; anonymous or named helper identity changes cannot create a new original B.
At least one such fresh extension is a mandatory generated RE01 duty in the complete local closure.

That closure cannot discard X in a child: fix one supporting M introduced by the first root, whose declaration and declared-type reference every descendant retains.
This M remains a fresh legality root even after filtering removes it from effective(H); its declaration, affiliation and type ancestry must still be checked, and any legal affiliation reaches exact B.
B consumes original A, whose unchanged z declaration consumes exact X, so retained X is required again in every locally qualified descendant.

The same anchored argument therefore yields at least one nonprimitive duty at every required candidate node.
It does not say that every arbitrary step adds a new helper: a context with several live supporters can rebase one while another remains live.
It says that no locally qualified required closure is a finite-proof leaf.

Any finite well-founded evidence graph has a sink among its required construction nodes.
At that sink the mandatory nonprimitive duty needs another exact child, contradicting the absence of an outgoing required premise.
Hence no permitted finite acyclic certificate with the current justified primitive leaves establishes this root, independently of helper count, search cutoff or tested trace length.

#### Alternative audit

| Proposed escape | Reason it does not supply a finite leaf |
| --- | --- |
| A live, wildcard or required-empty-choice suffix | Surviving outer unit range fails X's optional range |
| An erased suffix or pointless wrapper | Restores the same anchored H/Q comparison |
| A property-equal prefix copy | Preserves global H, exact named B/X references and fixed local Q/H names/flags |
| A fresh global named H or Q | Original global QName uniqueness forbids impersonation |
| A member typed by exact B | Identity makes it effective under Q as well as H |
| Restriction or mixed type ancestry | H blocks any restriction method |
| Simple or union type exceptions | The required exact target B is complex |
| Fresh affiliation bridges or anonymous helpers | Preserve the same exact B reachability and method exclusions |
| Rebasing a supporting-path type through a fresh direct extension of A | Loses an inherited member's required exact B affiliation |
| Reusing B as child intermediate | Adds a restriction; a live supporter must still remain or be replenished |
| A vacuous U→B→A path | The existing primitive rule requires a direct extension of its designated ancestor |
| Filtering old members out of both groups | Retains their declaration/type roots and B/A/z:X dependencies |

These are primary-rule arguments over every permitted finite addition, not exhaustive sampling.
The first QName premise uses [NameAndTypeOK.1](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#rcase-NameAndTypeOK) before its both-global exception; membership uses [cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec), and raw construction uses [cos-particle-extend](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-particle-extend).
The direct primitive boundary and all accepted domain/authority versions remain unchanged.

#### An explicit locally productive family

For each natural i, define finite context Cᵢ retaining the root X restriction of unchanged B, with fresh globally named U₀…Uᵢ and M₀…Mᵢ.
Each Uⱼ has exact A content; Uⱼ restricts B when j < i, while Uᵢ extends B, and Mⱼ has exact declared type Uⱼ and direct affiliation to H.
All other original and inherited properties remain unchanged, with empty new type/declaration flags, AUs and wildcards.

Choose names by appending the natural-number decimal spelling to distinct reserved prefixes U and M; this supplies injective global identities disjoint from original A/B/R/X/H/Q.
Each Cᵢ is finite; no infinite component graph or completed infinite request is admitted.
The mathematical construction has no configured resource bound, and sufficiently long executed traces can correctly exhaust the existing engineering budgets.

In Cᵢ, effective(H) = {H,Mᵢ} and effective(Q) = {Q,H}.
All older Mⱼ affiliations remain legal under empty final sets even though their restriction-derived types are filtered from both groups.
X's anchored mapping is locally valid, B remains primitive, and Uᵢ is the sole nonprimitive generated duty.

A child for Uᵢ changes only its derivation to restriction of exact B, then introduces Uᵢ₊₁ extending B and Mᵢ₊₁ affiliated with H.
The resulting context is Cᵢ₊₁, with exact candidate correspondence and every inherited/fresh root rechecked.
Local construction follows inductively: all helper content comparisons use exact A, all affiliations reach exact B, the z delimiter preserves UPA and repeated names retain exact B/X type identities for EDC.

Thus every finite stage has a locally qualified next construction with another outstanding duty.
This explicit one-live family adds a helper at each step; the universal no-sink theorem needs only a live duty and does not impose that particular transition recipe on arbitrary candidates.
There is no claim that an executed finite prefix, a cyclic storage node or an infinite local sequence is a semantically successful RE01 witness.

#### The mathematical construction operator

Fix this source Γ and let a construction node record its exact parent, child context, target, intermediate and designated ancestor, with all local premises qualified independently of pending RE01 judgments.
Its finite frontier consists of every generated nonprimitive duty in the complete required closure; the existing direct primitive rules discharge only their own duties.
Actual Γ's complete original obligations are checked separately, with X represented by the root and B primitive in this example.
The original X(Γ) debt is not inserted again in each candidate frontier; consumed candidate X has a restriction edge and owes its local mapping check, while the displayed frontier contains only Uᵢ.

For a set S of such nodes, define Φ(S) to contain precisely the locally qualified nodes whose every frontier duty has a matching exact child node in S.
Φ is monotone and finitary: a node has finitely many duties, and a supporting choice uses finitely many children.
Leaves with no nonprimitive duties are Φ(∅); no additional primitive or cyclic acceptance axiom is inserted.

The least fixed point μΦ is exactly finite well-founded derivability for this local inference system; with the separately checked actual premises and sole actual X debt, this is the finite-certificate boundary for this fixture.
One direction is induction on finite proof height; conversely, finite frontiers let each node in Φ(⋃ₙΦⁿ(∅)) choose children below a finite maximum height, so the union of these finite stages is a fixed point.
Sharing exact completed children changes storage but not finite derivability, and configurable resource interruption supplies no membership in either fixed point.

Let S contain the displayed root C₀ construction and every displayed Cᵢ→Cᵢ₊₁ child construction.
The uniform local argument gives S ⊆ Φ(S), so these nodes belong to the greatest fixed point νΦ.
The universal no-sink theorem excludes every root construction from μΦ, including alternatives outside the displayed family.

This establishes a least/greatest fixed-point discriminator for the exact mathematical local-construction operator.
It does not identify either fixed point with the independent semantic-success definition, establish an RE01 witness, refute semantic finite-evidence adequacy or justify a general negative answer.
Choosing an SCC, a fixed point or a larger resource allowance in code would not prove that missing equivalence.
Deleting or renaming inherited supporters inside an exact child violates its retained-root correspondence, but this does not refute an existential representative quotient.
Such a quotient or a guarded proof mechanism still needs sound extraction/reconstruction and an independently justified semantic inference principle; finite trace similarity cannot supply them.

The accepted domain permits investigating additional sufficient grounds rather than treating the current direct-only rule as complete.
The [following exact-vacuous counterexample](#exact-vacuous-extension-ground-fails-with-a-retained-consumer) refutes an unconditional ground based on U→B→A and exact content/AU equality, even when every parent local premise passes.
A narrower renewal-specific theorem must account for replaceable support and every B-v1 ambient contextual duty; the [global formal-model result](#global-formal-models-and-renewal-nonentailment) below shows that a positive ground cannot follow from the audited formalized constraints alone.

Each stage has precisely Uᵢ as its nonprimitive duty; even filtered inherited helpers' construction receipts retain exact X type dependencies, and truncated evidence reports `missing-nested-evidence` at the final Uᵢ.

If every semantic success entails a finite primitive-leaf derivation, this family supplies a semantic negative; if a semantic success can be proved outside μΦ, the finite verifier needs an adequate additional proof mechanism.
