#### Qualify NOTATION at datatype constituent formation

Direct builtin NOTATION used as a list item or union member previously escaped the declared-datatype guard.
That guard inspected only the selected plan's top atomic primitive; a composite plan could construct, describe and assess successfully even when one of its constituent uses was forbidden.
The independently reproduced pre-repair complete one-node forest checked all six actual components, including both invalid original constructors; removing their attribute consumers still checked all four remaining actual components.

##### Dated rule and existing owner contract

The dated [Datatypes NOTATION constraint](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-2-20041028/#enumeration-required-notation) states: “It is an error for NOTATION to be used directly in a schema. Only datatypes that are derived from NOTATION by specifying a value for enumeration can be used in a schema.”
The pinned primary XML has this mandatory clause at lines 4798–4807, following its current-schema notation-QName universe at lines 4784–4796.
Its SHA256 is `430f010df8ed077a4ed11d9d5812ea5e5d62ba67e715f1bbeeaa8ee1765d84c7`.

An item/member selecting bare NOTATION is a direct datatype use, including in an unused constructor.
The separate attribute/target-namespace compatibility advice is not the premise for this correction.
The existing [schema-only DT owner contract](content-model-s06-dt-01.md#shared-source-staging-in-architecture-recovery) already requires usable declared datatypes while permitting private primitive assessment to construct enumeration operands.
This repairs an implementation omission under that contract; it introduces no new B-v1 exclusion or P1/F2 policy.

##### Constructor boundary and preserved primitive roles

The [scalar formation owner](../src/compiler/scalarSchemaAssessment.ts) qualifies each formed list item's and union member's immediate description before publishing or caching the composite.
An atomic NOTATION constituent needs an effective enumeration layer; bottom-up formation applies the same rule to nested source, raw and original-generated composite descriptions.
A later outer enumeration cannot legalize the already invalid underlying constructor.

Builtin NOTATION remains available as an atomic restriction base and private typed enumeration operand.
The check runs on the constituent-use edge even when another valid enum restriction has already cached the primitive description in the same H.
Original/active facet operand ownership, exact generated-slot identity, ordered member selection, formation dependencies and shared request accounting retain their existing owners.

##### Independent source and complete evidence controls

The [research controls](../test/research/re01/notation-constituent-use.test.ts) reject bare source item/member uses, unused actual constructors, nested offending uses and an outer enum that selects only a different branch.
Fresh raw list and union constructors over a fully qualified Γ reject in their own H after same-context private NOTATION cache priming.
Invalid original-generated list/union properties remain rejected in their exact original context when accessed through H.

Positive controls retain atomic Q restricted from NOTATION with enum N, qualified list/ordered-union nesting and private primitive enum construction.
Their complete one-node forests check every actual member and generated scalar slot, and the fresh raw positive checks every added composite root.
Existing direct-declared and missing-notation negatives remain separate.

The [reference controls](../test/conformance/reference/re01-notation-constituent-use.test.ts) pin both explicit source constructor edges and the qualified source shape.
Pinned `libxml2-wasm 0.7.2 / libxml2 2.15.1` accepts both the qualified and bare source fixtures.
The bare-source acceptance is recorded as a contrary external observation; it does not override the independently reviewed dated use constraint or certify hypothetical RE01 evidence.

This correction does not establish NOTATION empty-template normalization, namespace-map erasure, a universal representation theorem or a local encoded-size bound.
Full coverage, complete negatives, termination, dated correspondence and #234/#232 acceptance remain open.

#### External methods and applicability

The inspected precedents show how others handle recursive definitions, but none supplies an automatic dated-XSD truth rule.
The subsequent [reviewed C² follow-up](https://github.com/TechSpokes/typescript-wsdl-client/issues/234#issuecomment-6274763459) records a positive result for its restricted logical skeleton with explicit domain and reconstruction limits.
Neither a failed direct decoder nor that scoped positive settles full-B representation or a general bound.

| Primary source | Actual result or practice | Transfer requirement for B |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Dagnino, corrected bounded-fixed-point account](https://lmcs.episciences.org/5277/pdf), Proposition 3.6 | Greatest fixed point below a supplied prefixed bound | N(K)⊆K is proved; K remains additional semantic structure, not an XSD conclusion |
| [Denecker/Bogaerts/Vennekens, inductive definitions](https://www.bartbogaerts.eu/articles/future/s001/SafeInductions.pdf), Definitions 3.16–3.17/Proposition 3.20 | Monotone inductive constructions yield the least closed structure | Establish inductive meaning; positivity alone does not select it or preserve primitive cycles |
| [Bogaerts/Jakubowski, ICLP2021](https://www.bartbogaerts.eu/articles/2021/006-ICLP-RecursiveSHACL/RecursiveSHACL.pdf), Theorem 4.1/Section 4 | AFT derives several semantics after explicit order/evaluation choices | Preserve B's additional grounds and state the chosen interpretation |
| [Okulmus/Šimkus, KR2024](https://proceedings.kr.org/2024/52/kr2024-0052-okulmus-et-al.pdf), Definition 3/Proposition 3/Section 7 | ShaWell translates chosen founded validation to propositional logic programs | Finite graph/shape atoms differ from unbounded exact construction contexts |
| [Oudshoorn/Ortiz/Šimkus, KR2026](https://proceedings.kr.org/2026/50/kr2026-0050-oudshoorn-et-al.pdf), Theorems 3–8/Proposition 5 | ALCIO founded static analysis via hybrid μ-calculus and automata | Prove a finite effective encoding and both candidate reconstruction directions |
| [Barguñó et al., TABG](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1302.6960), Definition 3.2, Sections 4.1–4.2 and Theorem 5.22 | Decidable tree emptiness with global subtree equality and restricted natural counting thresholds | Prove nominal sharing/context reconstruction and stay within the actual arithmetic syntax |
| [XMLmind implementation limits](https://www.xmlmind.com/xmleditor/_distrib/doc/xmltool/xsd_structure_limitations.html) | Explicitly lists cos-ct-extends.1.5 as unchecked | Omission is not a solution or permitted weakening here |
| [libxml2 c79c961](https://github.com/GNOME/libxml2/blob/c79c9612fcc77b06b0d16f35fd363d938bdbbf5e/xmlschemas.c#L16028) | Exact clause 1.5 remains TODO in the extension checker | Current-source omission is not a semantic theorem or a new reference-engine verdict |
| [goxsd8 a5c3269](https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/blob/a5c3269c20c26aed63c2940414cf43d97b8d54c5/xsd/complexextension.go#L444), [approximation issue 586](https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/586) | Checks a collapsed, unregistered throwaway intermediate | Does not supply coherent D′ and all B-v1 inherited-root validity duties |

KR2026's unrestricted implication result is EXPTIME-complete; finite implication is stated decidable without that tight bound.
ALCO has the cited finite-model property while ALCI does not, so the paper does not justify inferring a finite B witness from a generic fixed-point model.
Drawing a graph of judgments/candidate edges does not encode candidate coherence, exact names/identities, raw particle/AU/scalar rules or retained-root conditions in its restricted syntax.

The investigator inspected the cited definitions, applicability and algorithm sections, rather than every appendix or cited work.
Dagnino's corrected Proposition 3.6 justifies the bounded fixed-point construction; its descending approximation results require additional continuity assumptions, not merely finite rule premises.
No coaxiom is inferred that makes every original Γ judgment true: R erases selected premises only in the auxiliary analysis.
The coordinator's positive investigator inspected the pinned libxml2/goxsd8 functions and documented code boundaries, without executing those toolchains or auditing their entire implementations.
The official [Saxon 13 conformance page](https://www.saxonica.com/html/documentation13/conformance/schema11.html) documents use of XSD 1.1 derivation rules even for an XSD 1.0 request; that version policy is not correspondence with this dated relation.
The 1991 well-founded-semantics author PDF had unusable extracted text, and the WWW2020 stable-model full PDF retrieval failed; neither uninspected proof is used.
No whole-web absence or general undecidability conclusion follows.

The TABG continuation inspected the defining equality semantics, arithmetic boundary and pumping theorem through coordinator-supplied primary excerpts; it did not audit the whole paper.
Global state-pair equality compares whole subterms, not properties conditionally joined by an unbounded nominal helper ID.
Its decidable arithmetic uses nonnegative counting coefficients and constant thresholds; arbitrary cross-state count comparisons combined with subtree equality are outside that result and have an undecidability theorem for that automaton formalism.
No reduction from that hard formalism to immutable B-v1 inputs was established.

Strict owner-executed controls also distinguish exact raw mappings from language equivalence: pointwise unrolling of a fresh repeated compositor can reject an otherwise complete forest while all original particles remain fixed.
Another complete forest realizes a shared fresh sequence with exact x repetitions and y-occurrence wildcard content; separate restriction consumers bind x, y and their effective-total-range product.
The product and each binding mismatch retain their own actual full-checker outcomes.
That optional fresh support can be omitted, so these controls establish neither RE01 hardness nor failure of a SOME normalization.
The earlier fixed-layout arithmetic result keeps raw compositor counts constant and is unaffected.

The next implementation route is exact candidate/frontier materialization and effective finite-forest search for Q_f, with a separately proved input-derived bound or equivalent complete-negative method before total claims.
Normative correspondence, arbitrary runtime representation, general coverage, complete negatives and mathematical termination remain open.
No release, production activation, negative API or downstream #179/#153/#184 ownership change follows from selecting this research relation.

### No complete F child for the fixed membership-loss parent

This continuation starts from #308 at fetched main `cc81f01241c80d808c6c38977fedb2d09bd9f7cb`, tree `4dfdf3636657e68fe8fc992058e475ad9cc80750`.
Investigation/implementation began at `2026-10-19T07:17:00Z`, with cutoff `2026-10-19T08:47:00Z`; #234 records the earlier finding freeze and subsequent review/checks/delivery.
The question quantifies over every permitted complete F child of the fixed parent, including retained or fresh intermediates and arbitrary permitted finite additions under `RE01-derivation-B-v1`.

#### Fixed context and theorem

Fix either admitted original or repaired #308 parent from the preceding subsection, with exact p=M.type=F, q=H.type=E, F initially extending E, H.final empty and H.block containing restriction.
H and M are the retained nonabstract global declarations with distinct names disjoint from original local a; F and E are distinct exact named complex definitions.
Keep all other supplied records and obligations, including R_H restricting E, R_M restricting R_H, their distinct wrappers/required uses, exact shared A.content and required empty choice C.

There is no permitted complete F child of this fixed parent.
This is a contextual nonexistence theorem about inherited obligations, not a general-negative decision procedure or a claim that no different Γ-root witness exists.
The proof uses necessary local premises only; failure occurs before nested evidence for any newly added extension could repair the child.

#### Reusable first-edge obstruction

In the fixed parent, F's immediate base E differs from its designated ancestor A, whose immediate base is anyType.
Thus the accepted primitive-no-change rule does not apply to F.
The B-v1 nontrivial construction requires the endpoint retaining exact F identity to restrict a complex intermediate I which extends exact A; a vacuous restriction still has that restriction edge.

`re01CandidateCorrespondence.ts:checkCandidateCorrespondence` owns these two direct edges and preserves the endpoint's effective non-derivation properties.
`schemaConstructionAssessment.ts` identifies F's nonprimitive obligation, while `finiteRe01Verification.ts` admits primitive identity only when the immediate base is the designated ancestor.
This uses the accepted B-v1 construction interpretation of [cos-ct-extends.1.5](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-ct-extends); it adds no new blanket interpretation of the primary composition/vacuity note.

For any such child, exact F remains distinct from exact E.
By [Type Derivation OK (Complex)](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-ct-derived-ok), a successful F-to-E derivation must follow F's base ancestry and includes its first restriction edge.
If that ancestry misses E, it cannot establish F-to-E derivation at all.

The exact-identity alternative cannot remove this first edge: equal content, a reused particle, or a fresh equal-property type is not identity with E.
The primary [identity note](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#no-identity) and accepted exact identity contract keep the two named definitions distinct; adding globals cannot alias either existing type.
`schemaTypeIdentity.ts:resolveSemanticType` and `schemaTypeRelations.ts:relationStep` implement those exact identities rather than content equality.

The simple-type exceptions supply no bypass.
F, I and E are complex; F's restriction cannot have a simple base under [ct-props-correct.2](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#ct-props-correct), and E retains element-only complex content.
Even if a later ancestry step could enter simple types, [Type Derivation OK (Simple)](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-st-derived-ok) offers identity, simple-base recursion, the simple ur-type and a union target's members; none reaches this distinct named complex E without encountering E itself.

In particular E is neither anySimpleType nor a simple union target.
The owner `schemaTypeRelations.ts:substitutionTypes` first qualifies type derivation, retains accumulated complex-prefix methods and blocks, and considers union alternatives only for a union target.
No later method, added helper or simple/union alternative erases the initial restriction.

Consequently [Substitution Group OK (Transitive).2.3](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec) excludes M from effective(H), because restriction belongs to H.block and to every successful F-to-E method set.
M and H are distinct declarations, so the same-element exception does not apply either.
Additional entered type blocks or substitution in H.block can only add an exclusion.

This reusable obstruction concerns effective membership, not immediate affiliation.
With empty H.final, an I retaining an F-to-E path can keep M's affiliation legal under [e-props-correct.4](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#e-props-correct); an I whose path misses E fails that separate obligation as well.
`schemaTypeRelations.ts:checkSubstitution` keeps affiliation and substitutability separate, and `effectiveSubstitutionMembers` applies the latter plus the nonabstract filter.

#### Consumer mapping under arbitrary finite additions

The preceding reusable argument alone does not prove the consumer comparison fails: #308's original mapping lemma assumed its stated incoming-affiliation inventory.
Now allow any permitted finite declarations and affiliations under H, under M or elsewhere, and recompute both effective groups in the child.
If any addition violates its own declaration/type/construction duties, the child already fails; assume those duties hold when considering a possible mapping repair.

Nonabstract retained M is always in effective(M), by the same-declaration alternative of cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec and [cos-equiv-class](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-equiv-class).
Thus the derived required m1 position either remains the M element or becomes a required implicit choice with M as one of its leaves.
Fresh affiliates can add leaves; they cannot remove that mandatory M leaf, make M abstract, change its type or affiliation, or lower m1's occurrence range.

The base required h1 position either remains H or becomes a required implicit choice of effective(H).
It contains no M leaf by the first-edge obstruction.
Every such virtual leaf is a global declaration: [e-props-correct.3](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#e-props-correct) requires global scope for an affiliated declaration.
[sch-props-correct.2](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#sch-props-correct) and B-v1 global uniqueness prohibit another declaration with M's expanded QName, including a fresh affiliate intended to impersonate M; a fresh local declaration cannot join that group.

The fixed outer sequences normalize to three positions: optional original local a, required H/M or its implicit choice, and C.
New globals cannot affiliate to or rewrite local a; its distinct QName cannot accept M.
C remains a required empty choice under [cos-particle-restrict.2.2](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#cos-particle-restrict), so it has no M target; formal emptiability or an empty instance language is not a component-mapping exception.

The distinct sequence roots and required use particles cannot use cos-particle-restrict.1's same-particle shortcut.
Exact A.content/C sharing permits equality for those operands only; adding particles cannot replace or alias the inherited middle uses.
`particleSemanticView.ts` retains these authenticated identities, flattens the unit A sequence, and virtualizes declarations without unfolding their declared type content.
Its fresh virtual leaves carry no `originalParticle`, so expansion of both middle choices cannot introduce a shared original-particle shortcut.

Both normalized outer sequences have three explicit positions.
[Recurse](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#rcase-Recurse) requires a complete strictly order-preserving functional mapping; with these equal three-position lists, the middle position must map to the middle position.
Within that pair, element-to-choice uses [RecurseAsIfGroup](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#rcase-RecurseAsIfGroup) and then [RecurseLax](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#rcase-RecurseLax); choice-to-choice uses RecurseLax directly.

RecurseLax maps every derived leaf, including M, to a base leaf.
[NameAndTypeOK.1](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#rcase-NameAndTypeOK) requires matching expanded names before its both-global-declarations exception, so M has no base target.
If new affiliates virtualize M but leave H an element, choice-to-element has no permitted restriction dispatch; if both remain elements, their distinct names fail directly.

The accepted implicit-order interpretation permits some coherent enumeration, but no ordering creates a missing QName or omits a derived leaf.
Fresh choice alternatives cannot cross the surrounding explicit sequence positions; pointless unit-choice removal preserves its surviving leaf and does not delete M.
`particleRestriction.ts` implements these complete mappings and coherent order choices; failure is independent of their enumeration order or cardinalities.

There is no wildcard in either consumer comparison.
E's retained tail is an operand of R_H-to-E, not R_M-to-R_H; a new affiliate's declared type wildcard is not an element-choice alternative or an inserted consumer particle.
New groups, particles, AUs, scalar helpers and other additions have no permission to rewrite the inherited consumer content or references, so no wildcard rule supplies a bypass.

#### Complete-child consequence and controls

R_M and R_H remain fresh relative to immutable Γ and are inherited mandatory legality roots in every F child, along with their particles and dependencies.
The [finite verifier](../src/compiler/finiteRe01Verification.ts) enumerates all such inherited roots relative to Γ, not merely the current child delta; this is also an obligation of semantic success independently of the evidence format.
Their original-context or parent-context receipts cannot discharge the child-context comparison.

Therefore R_M's particle fails [derivation-ok-restriction.5.4](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#derivation-ok-restriction) in every proposed complete child.
This contradicts a necessary local duty before considering any further nested existential evidence.
The conclusion quantifies over all permitted finite additions without a helper bound, enumeration argument, evidence-adequacy assumption or cross-context receipt transfer.

| --- | --- | --- |
| Nonprimitive F; restriction-first correspondence | Construction/correspondence | Retained E and fresh I; unchanged-edge rejection |
| Effective membership versus affiliation | Type relations/declarations | First restriction excluded; fresh I misses E |
| Both choices may grow; retained M cannot disappear | Effective groups/particle views | Direct H and direct/transitive M affiliates |
| Missing QName defeats every implicit order | Restriction/QName identity | Equal-size virtual choices; varied names/insertion order |
| Exact identity is a real exception | Type identity/type relations | Existing equal-declared-type stable-membership controls |
| Inherited mandatory duties and full positive contrast | Finite verifier | Existing descendant rejection and complete stable positives |

The [typed controls](../test/research/re01/live-helper-forest.test.ts) extend existing tests through these owners; the [independent clause traces](../test/conformance/reference/re01-particle-continuation.test.ts) import no semantic owner for their expectations.
The nonauthor reviewer independently challenges the universal argument and constructs separate contrasts before reliance, then reviews final proof/code and scoped #232 consistency.
The complete pinned Structures XML was re-read with editorial deletions excluded; SHA-256 is `e496af408b14853e6169ac7c1fca09d55a81bd73771758ad6be020960e1317ba`.

The official second-edition errata, re-read October 19, contains only Part 1 E1-56's default/PSVI correction and no amendment to these clauses.
The consumers retain empty AU/attribute-wildcard properties, and original string/int operands remain unchanged; AU C1, PW R-240, DT A, #178, GSC1 and P1/F2 keep their accepted scopes.
There is no new production obstruction API, rule owner, verifier authority, search strategy, budget or activation change.

Existing complete witnesses for other fresh inventories over the same integer-restoration Γ remain valid, including the stable-membership consumer roots and the earlier forest without this membership trap.
Constructing such a different Γ-root may choose different fresh declarations/flags or no consumers; it does not supply a child of this fixed parent, whose inherited nonendpoint records cannot be omitted or rewritten.
The theorem establishes neither general evidence inadequacy nor a complete general-negative procedure.

Full #234/#232 acceptance remains open for arbitrary-success coverage, general contextual evidence adequacy, copied-prefix transport, input-derived bounds or an equivalent terminating negative criterion, and mathematical general search termination.

## Identity allocation and remaining reconstruction obligations

This continuation starts from actual main `f8e0368fb32ac8caf131d50695b7dde33eddb0d2`, tree `d3e878f75863377c1379a09c23fad581cddb2f17`.
It preserves B-v1, the selected founded research relation, all seven accepted recovery contracts and P1/F2.
The implementation removes a finite identity-allocation obstruction; it does not supply the missing successful-construction bound.

### Fresh scoped identities and complete stored reservations

Actual source and global identities keep their existing spelling.
A genuinely fresh scoped hypothetical component may additionally use the exact JSON tuple `['hypothetical-scoped-v1', owner, path, role]`.
The existing admission owner checks the complete tuple; an inherited endpoint replacement must retain both its exact stored ID and its exact semantic identity.

The tuple encoding is injective on its UTF-16 fields: equality of the serialized arrays implies equality of each field.
This argument does not assume that the legacy finite hash is injective.
Legacy hypothetical spellings remain admitted, but two spellings of one semantic tuple cannot coexist.

Reservations include the entire immutable original graph and all stored changes, including source-only originals and inactive inherited records.
Duplicate admission is checked before any private-content query is needed.
The existing native-ID original index is prepared once, and each immutable context has its own completed-only lazy change index; a sibling cannot supply another context's reservations.

For fixed owner and role, consider paths `/$hypothetical/0` through `/$hypothetical/N`, where N is the sum of the sizes of the forbidden-ID and forbidden-tuple sets.
These N+1 candidates have pairwise distinct tuples and pairwise distinct injective IDs.
Each forbidden ID or tuple excludes at most one candidate, so at least one candidate is available.

The allocator checks the finite forbidden sets and returns such an identity.
For an already finite acyclic ownership cohort, allocate owners before their dependent declarations and extend the forbidden sets after each allocation.
This proves finite fresh allocation for that supplied cohort; it does not bound the cohort or its encoded size from the input.

Every variable-length tuple is charged before serialization or publication, including escaping and recursively encoded owner lengths.
Lookup, storage and comparisons remain charged; exhaustion is sticky and supplies no nonexistence result.
The actual/source admission format is unchanged, and a tag string is not authority to issue a semantic view.

### Private effective-content and ur-type identities

The two private backing-particle issuers now use `['semantic-private-particle-v1', family, owner, path, role]`.
The family distinguishes actual effective-content particles from builtin anyType particles.
No receiving context, selected outer type or per-view nonce is added to the actual source tuple.

Thus equal source tuples retain equal private IDs across ancestor/descendant mappings, while different complete tuples have different IDs.
Real source particle IDs, declaration IDs, child incidence, occurrence ranges and native ur-wildcard markers stay exact.
Injectively renaming the private particle coordinate preserves equality of coherent particle/head order keys.

Raw original handles, property projections and authenticated subtrees reuse the actual backing identities.
Their private WeakMap/request/root provenance remains authoritative; copied wrappers and guessed strings remain insufficient.
Every predicate is checked in its required current context, without relabelling a Gamma validity receipt as a hypothetical receipt.

A full encoding-independent tuple lookup is required after private IDs become tagged.
Checking the new private string alone would miss a legacy stored component with the same owner/path/role tuple.
The immutable native-ID original index and final-change tuple index jointly cover that case even in an otherwise all-legacy context.
Every original passed exact legacy-ID admission, so a given scoped tuple can occupy only its computed legacy ID.
A direct lookup followed by full semantic-identity comparison finds that occupant without conflating distinct tuples that share a legacy hash.

Lookup scope and rejection policy stay separate.
Actual effective-content and builtin issuance reject active occupation, preserving their existing inactive-only permission.
Original imports, projections and handle admission reject any stored occupation, including outside-context records.

The appended private rows in `ParticleContentView.graph` are diagnostic backing rows, not newly admitted canonical members or persisted catalog entries.
Private tags are rejected as actual components, hypothetical additions and endpoint IDs.
No canonical-source validator is relaxed to accept them.

For genuinely source-built Gamma, relative canonical paths are slash-separated decimal source indexes or `/`.
They cannot be private `$semantic-content/` or `$semantic-builtin-anyType/` paths.
Choosing all reconstructed scoped additions in the separate `/$hypothetical/` family also prevents future private semantic-tuple aliases.

The distinct tags separately prevent string collisions with component IDs.
Consequently this chosen finite source-derived reconstruction has no hash-range obstruction at later private issuance.
An arbitrary caller-supplied parent containing a genuine reserved tuple alias still correctly rejects; universal incoming availability is not claimed.

### Measured preparation and query locality

Two unnecessary full original tuple indexes were rejected during qualification.
Building that index at the first content query made 2,048 unrelated originals add 732,496 work units and failed the existing locality control.
Moving it into preparation restored query locality but made Weather preparation use 1,082,493 work units, failing its unchanged 1,000,000 control in full CI.

The final implementation reuses exact legacy-ID admission and direct original lookup instead, with a charged full-tuple comparison after a hit.
Only final changes need the lazy dual-encoding tuple index; every stored original remains covered without another source scan or preparation index.
Weather now prepares in 988,108 work units, and the complete author profile uses 934,832 for the unchanged 950,000 control.
The existing 990,000 nested control, unrelated-original query control and complete legacy/injective two-node forests also pass.
Independent qualification rechecks these corrected-source contracts, including the previously missed source-preparation suites.
These are measured engineering controls, not mathematical bounds on the accepted witness domain.

### Ownership depth and unused particle support

Fresh ownership depth has a separate conditional normalization.
Fix qualified Gamma, authentic parent P, a complete finite forest and one actual Gamma component D available throughout that forest.
Keep protected original/P identities and non-derivation fields fixed.
The permitted endpoint method/base replacement may refer to a freshly renamed intermediate through the same coherent renaming.
Every explicit semantic source, type, scalar and evidence selector retains its selected meaning; original bindings and lexical contexts are not reassigned.

At first birth, a local element or attribute is reowned by its old first complex-type, group, attribute-group or global scope stop.
Every other fresh scoped component is reowned by D.
Apply one coherent fresh-ID renaming to genuine semantic references, preserving every old complex scope stop and every absent/global scope category.

The simultaneous declaration rewrite is essential.
Resetting only an intermediate owner's edge can change a declaration's scope from absent to a complex type.
Keeping the old stopping component directly as the declaration's owner avoids that change.

Every new declaration then reaches an immutable stop in one edge, or a fresh nondeclaration whose next edge reaches D.
If H(P,Gamma) bounds immutable ownership depth, total new depth is at most H(P,Gamma)+2.
Rebuilding the entire forest from Gamma and normalizing every birth gives the input-derived corollary H(Gamma)+2 in every retained context.

Explicit facet operand owners follow their selected old semantic record under the coherent renaming; they are not reset to D.
Original bindings, generated scalar authorities, namespace maps, lexical contexts and source recipes remain qualified in their existing owners.
Private current-context views are reissued authentically; no receipt is transferred across contexts.

The noncontainment type/base/content/source reference graph, particle sharing and coherent order-key equality patterns remain isomorphic.
Declaration scope comparisons retain their exact stopping components; the containment-owner edges themselves are intentionally shortened.
The old finite forest can therefore be replayed under that renaming, pruning only duties no longer consumed while retaining every inherited fresh root and independent actual Gamma obligation.
This is an ownership-depth result, not a bound on component count, field count, group width, scope anchors or contexts.

After this normalization, a fresh particle need not remain merely as another component's containment owner.
An unused closed particle-only scaffold can be omitted before birth; if it first becomes needed later, introduce its old current record at that later birth and replay the supported current-context closure.
All nonparticle selectors, group/content ports and inherited particles remain protected, and the full reference/frontier audit is still necessary.

### Local decisions still need a complete successful inventory

The preceding stipulated finite-census local content result requires a supplied qualified nominal/scalar/source inventory and its source/identity/key interpretation.
It does not independently compare two freely chosen content blocks or bound how many such blocks an arbitrary successful construction needs.
Counting only extension/restriction method masks does not connect nonadjacent retained payloads.

Follow-on local infinity, incoming fixed-derived and exact-frontier constructions require their own complete integration and qualification.
They are not silently supplied by this identity implementation.

### A complete forest refutes frozen-prefix transport

The [two-namespace source](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/recovery/frozen-prefix-transport.xsd) and its [import](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/recovery/frozen-prefix-transport-inside.xsd) have fourteen actual components.
B is a primitive extension of A with an inside-namespace wildcard; T0 extends B with a dead suffix containing a required empty choice followed by optional global Hs.
The outside original G has type T0 and affiliates with Hs; the inside original M has type B and also affiliates with Hs.

Hs blocks restriction, so G is effective while T0 extends B and is filtered when T0 restricts B.
Its fixed global identity and type keep that change independent of any added member.
T0 has complete native UPA/EDC, but its content does not restrict B in the parent: the namespace check reaches outside G even beyond the dead choice.

The complete positive adds U restricting T0 with empty content, V extending U with empty content and N of type V affiliated with the fixed head H_B of type B.
The endpoint D restricts existing B.
Its six-node finite assessment forest supplies the separate actual T0 proof and both retirement orders for the main context's T0 and V duties.

Every actual component is covered and every one of U, V and N remains checked in all five main proof contexts.
This is complete evidence rather than a locally successful candidate with missing children.
No original validity receipt is reused in a changed context.

Now prescribe exact T0 content for both U and V at their first birth, keeping their identities and base path.
Both parent constructions check, and the T0-first branch has the expected legal local checks.
The V-first child fails `derivation-ok-restriction.5.4`: its middle must be exact B, while inherited T0 is still an extension and G remains visible outside B's wildcard namespace.

The exact middle is forced by N's persistent affiliation to H_B:B.
The child's intermediate directly extends A, and reaching unchanged B on that path therefore requires the intermediate to be B itself.
Finite additions cannot remove immutable G or insert a restriction into unchanged T0's path before that child's check.

A legal raw extension of the prescribed T0 prefix must retain its property-identical global Hs occurrence.
The global identity cannot be replaced by a local same-name declaration, and the retained G remains in that occurrence's expansion.
The native wildcard rule inspects it despite the required empty choice, so no permitted finite additions repair this frozen-prefix prescription in the V-first child.

The [two controls](../test/research/re01/frozen-prefix-transport.test.ts) preserve the complete old forest and the exact failed recook.
The U/V/N cone is unused by the host D and can be omitted before birth.
Thus this refutes that prescribed transport, not general SOME reconstruction, a different common payload, executable evidence adequacy or a complete decision procedure.

### Fully formed restriction chains are not transitive

The [same-context source](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/recovery/restriction-nontransitivity.xsd) has twelve actual components and no generated nonprimitive obligation.
A is a primitive extension of empty Z with an ordinary required `##any`/`skip` wildcard; B restricts A with a unit choice of distinct local a:string and b:string; D restricts B with a required empty choice.
The ordinary wildcard is not the distinguished builtin ur-type wildcard.

All twelve actual roots pass full finite verification, with zero evidence nodes and only primitive A recorded as no-change.
UPA, EDC, source syntax, exact occurrence ranges and both immediate restrictions are qualified in one actual context.
No changed substitution census or independent ordering assignment is used to explain the result.

Restriction normalization leaves D as a required empty choice and B as its two-member choice, while A's unit sequence normalizes to its wildcard.
D restricts B because the choice branch has no derived children and its root range is 1..1.
B restricts A because its formal total range is 1..1 and every element namespace is admitted.

D does not restrict A: the wildcard branch compares its formal total range 0..0 with A's required minimum 1 and rejects.
D is formally schema-emptiable but nonnullable and unrealizable; those separate facts do not change the selected restriction branches.
Thus two positive, fully formed adjacent restriction steps cannot generally be replaced by one direct positive particle comparison.

The [native control](../test/research/re01/restriction-nontransitivity.test.ts) checks all actual roots and the exact true/true/false tuple on authenticated views from the same context.
This disproves unconditional particle-restriction transitivity, not a SOME normal form or a bounded interpolation theorem.
It establishes neither unbounded minimum bridge length nor undecidability.

### Token capacity of a supplied scalar support closure

Fix qualified P, a supplied finite scalar dependency/observer skeleton and finitely many positive initial assessments whose actual values form V.
These are ordinary retained values or protected P facet operands; gratuitous new facet queries are not initial anchors.
Keep complete formation, F2, namespace/source qualification and every mandatory frontier duty as premises.

Expand a positive assessment through its actual selected union member and list items, retaining failed earlier attempts where needed.
For a successful new restriction value, choose its qualified immediate-base witness with the same actual family and value.
The following computable K bounds the token count of every LIST attempt in this closure, including failed attempts.

| Value family in V | Token bound for any spelling of that fixed value |
| --- | --- |
| list | Exact number of items |
| string or anyURI | Token count after XML-whitespace collapse of the canonical string |
| base64Binary | Canonical nonspace base64 length, 4 times ceiling(byte length divided by 3) |
| Other admitted atomic family | One |

Take the maximum of these quantities and 1.
The base64 quantity is the re-encoded base64 length, not the stored hexadecimal canonical length.
Every nonempty token uses at least one of those fixed nonspace characters, so arbitrary inserted XML whitespace cannot exceed that bound.

Selected union edges and successful immediate-base witnesses preserve their anchored actual value.
Any earlier LIST member attempt shares that same whole lexical word, so the family bound applies even when the list attempt fails.
Its item assessments each receive one whitespace-free token.

New auxiliary values can arise in these item assessments, including earlier failed item-union attempts.
A qualified item union and every corresponding union on its base path have only atomic members; an atomic corresponding member cannot derive a list target.
Consequently these auxiliary branches create no further LIST attempt.

An auxiliary binary value can genuinely need a spaced union-base spelling because an earlier atomic member shadows its token spelling.
That base path remains atomic-member-only, so this exception does not create an unbounded token/list branch.
Source union flattening and raw constructor qualification must both be included; referring only to a source syntax name is insufficient.

This proof does not replace a list's pattern while keeping its enclosing union unchanged.
Such a local change can expose a later member's different typed value and violate F2; parsing also precedes the pattern check and may expose an unqualified branch.
The token bound avoids that unproved rewrite and retains the required old failed attempts.

K bounds list branching for the supplied positive support inventory.
It does not extract that inventory from Gamma, bound lexical word lengths, namespace maps, auxiliary atomic values or fresh scalar types, or decide their simultaneous realization.
Complete scalar SUCCESS and an input-derived scalar encoding bound remain open.

### Exact remaining closure plan

A total local bound b(Gamma,P,target), or an equivalent complete local criterion, must cover complete SUCCESS rather than just current local predicate truth.
It must supply faithful executable evidence, every inherited fresh root and every genuinely consumed actual/hypothetical duty.
The existing fresh-target final-record selection permits no newly added nonprimitive type at a fresh-target step; it does not remove mandatory inherited nonprimitive types.

Given such a computable local bound, the lexicographic measure counts unretired original nonprimitive types first and remaining inherited fresh nonprimitive types second.
Original-target steps decrease the first count and may add a bounded finite batch; fresh-target steps decrease the second count without adding a new batch.
Finite bounded candidate enumeration and induction on that measure would then justify terminating positive and negative answers.

The local bound has not been proved.
Open ingredients include a bounded useful global-member/nominal-role inventory, simultaneous payload interpolation across retained-type retirements, the joint scalar support-word realization bound, and executable frontier/materialization adequacy.
A supplied finite skeleton, successful finite experiment or rejected reconstruction does not discharge those universal claims.

The full dated-XSD correspondence gate remains separate from the selected founded research definition.
External engines are observations only: XMLmind's own [implementation-limit record](https://xmlmind.com/xmleditor/_distrib/doc/xmltool/xsd_structure_limitations.html) lists clause cos-ct-extends.1.5 among unchecked schema constraints and describes a different particle-restriction algorithm.
It therefore does not supply the missing decision theorem for this native contract.

General construction coverage, universal executable evidence adequacy, complete negative answers, mathematical termination and dated correspondence remain open.
No production activation, public format change, domain exclusion or semantic-budget reinterpretation follows from this continuation.

## Owner-conditional joint helper reconstruction

This continuation starts from verified main `b0aae81b844c502454eb0bc03f0647be7894801f`, tree `841225856bb12de6921e39aa3b2f6c2a957545ed`.
It supplies a complete-context reconstruction theorem for a stated unobserved family, not a finite bound on all successful constructions.
The theorem and its optional-value premise received separate nonauthor source scrutiny before the native before/after control was executed.

### Quantified input and observations

Fix source-qualified immutable Gamma, an authentic initial parent P and one supplied finite complete founded SUCCESS forest W.
Every node includes its required current-context full construction, every inherited fresh root, typed/scalar/source obligations and its separate actual-Gamma duties.
Assume faithful native reauthentication, authentic original handles/private views and enough configured resources for the rebuilt finite construction.
These representation premises are not discharged by checking local truth alone.

For every positive production raw-extension property query in W, record its owning complex extension z and its forced complex paired graph in that exact context and reification phase.
The production caller is [raw particle extension](../src/compiler/particleExtension.ts); arbitrary standalone equality calls do not become new construction obligations.
A successful query has a finite coinductive paired tableau, including recurrent pairs.

The only branching in [component-property comparison](../src/compiler/schemaComponentEquality.ts) is AU matching.
Qualified same-QName attribute sets force the same exact declaration pair, hence the same structural type/scope pairs, across its matching branches.
Lexical own-value pairings can differ; deterministic property readers already qualify their scalar TYPE/formation recipes.
Deleting a traversal can change the first memo visit, so the proof uses this forced structural closure rather than one incidental execution trace.

### Finite owner-conditional marking

Start with all Gamma/P complex identities and the active effective TYPE of each original/P declaration, including raw selectors.
Also seed every retained global element's complex declared type and base path, every retained source/scalar payload operand and every retained foreign middle/ancestor.
Unprojected scalar, simple-content, source and fixed-evidence ports remain seeds; a separately proved projection may justify removing a port, but the theorem does not assume one merely because the record is fresh.
Mandatory full checking of a newly born helper is not itself a seed for its old payload recipe.

Use one history-wide finite set of marks, with base-ancestor closure over every old W context.
Activate a recorded property query only when its owner z is marked.
Within an activated query, propagate a mark across a forced complex pair only when one operand is already marked; repeat with base closure to a fixed point.

This is conditional edge propagation.
An activated query may compare two unmarked fresh types; they can be rebuilt together.
An identity pair cannot mark a previously unmarked payload, because exact identity closes that comparison before its fields are opened.

Let X be the unmarked post-P complex identities born with method extension.
Let C include X and the unmarked birth-restriction types whose restriction-only base paths reach X in retained simulated nodes.
No C own-retirement branch is retained in the new forest.

C restriction bases stay in C.
An outside unmarked extension would belong to X, an outside unmarked restriction entering C would belong to its restriction cone, and a marked type cannot have an unmarked base ancestor.
Consequently outside base paths and retained global-element type paths avoid C.
Whole-history base closure also protects every retained outside target's exact old-child middle and ancestor; those are not chosen using the eventual C set.

For an activated positive query, a complex pair touching C has both operands in C.
A marked other operand would mark the first; for two unmarked operands, matching E/R methods and finite paired restriction paths reach unmarked extensions together.
This is a local closure argument on acyclic immediate-base paths, not an assumption that recursive content/property graphs are acyclic.

### Simultaneous first-birth construction

Choose one fresh global K, an empty restriction of builtin anyType with empty final/block, AUs and attribute wildcard.
Reserve its name and identity against the whole supplied finite W, including all later births and every stored source/inherited reservation; current-parent freshness alone is insufficient.
Introduce K at the first C birth on each retained branch and keep it thereafter.
Finite fresh identity allocation supplies this name; it does not bound the number of old components.

Keep every C identity, owner, path, QName, lexical context, namespace and abstract/final/block flag.
Keep every C birth method and every C restriction base edge; redirect only C extension bases to K.
At first birth choose empty canonical/raw content, attributes/AUs and wildcard for all C rows, with coherent content-construction fields.

Retain every old particle, declaration, AU, group, owner record and mandatory fresh root, even when its owner no longer selects that payload.
Outside raw roots, source/semantic selectors and real particle IDs remain exact.
Every C extension is primitive over K; every C restriction has only Q-free C support.
Empty/empty content/AU clauses, K's empty final and each unchanged legal R edge construct the family.

The complex restriction-only TypeDerivation answers used by NameAndTypeOK remain exact: R edges are unchanged and excluded E stops before recursion through the changed base.
Its eager immediate-base descriptor read can newly publish K; mandatory K/builtin construction covers that fact.
Global membership/affiliation, EDC identity/namedness, old particle order-key sharing and census capacities remain exact.
No old particle keys are merged into original keys.

### Optional local values and required frontiers

A mixed NP declaration, or a simple-content R descendant, can have a value constraint; particle-only invalidity is not a general value-erasure argument.
Erase only genuinely new C-typed local declaration values under the following joint presence projection.
Keep all original/P/global ordinary fields and all AU fields.

Use positive full-property equality edges only from activated queries; C-owner queries that disappear are not retained observations.
Activated C pairs have C on both sides, so paired values can be removed together.
For a positive fixed-value restriction implication into a C-typed declaration, its base type must be on the same restriction-only C segment before the excluded E; an outside fixed antecedent cannot enter this erased set.

Removing a fixed field can enable additional particle mapping states.
The proof preserves a successful mapping, not identical matching execution: newly reached real declarations, type/base facts, head census and scalar recipes require the complete consumed-operand closure in their owning context.
Property-only scope reads instead use the forced positive query graph; they are not promoted to full current-H validity.
With fully qualified operands and enough resources, an additional pending order path does not suppress the retained clear successful path, and no new fatal local input/schema error is introduced by weakening these fields.

If an input cannot establish that frontier/presence premise, keep its relevant value/scalar observer as a seed or leave this transformation unqualified.
Exhaustion or a missing qualification remains unresolved, never nonexistence.

### Queries that remain and queries that disappear

Every retained outside-C extension owner is marked: an unmarked post-P birth extension would be in X, while all original/P types were seeded.
Its new property query therefore maps to an activated old query in that node and phase, with unchanged outside content/base selectors.
Authentic source correspondence transports reencoded private wrappers down to their exact real declaration ports; it does not require literal equality of every wrapper ID.

In an activated query, outside pairs keep their fields and successor tasks.
C/R pairs keep their old base pair; C/E pairs replace their bases by K/K identity.
Both C contents/AU sets are empty, so the old C payload tasks disappear.
All namespace, identity, method and flag gates remain exact.

Changed memo order cannot create an unsupported structural pair: every new nonidentity task is in the old forced closure or the empty-C/K case.
The scalar formation recipes of outside AU alternatives were already read by the old positive query.
This is necessary because complex base comparisons are queued; their eventual mismatch does not prevent earlier LIFO attribute/content reads.

An unmarked C owner's own old query need not preserve its comparisons with marked original types.
Its reconstructed empty/empty extension closes before raw particle comparison, so that entire query disappears.
Independent full checking of its retained old payload roots does not restore the deleted owner query.

This distinction repairs the circular observation problem.
A previous original-only source-dominance approach had an open route through a retired original anonymous type's fresh primitive middle and borrowed content, exposing an original scope without full consuming it.
The owner-conditional construction does not require that stronger consumption claim; it can remove the self-owner query that made the borrowed scope relevant.

### Complete evidence reconstruction

Rebuild the complete conjunction at every retained node: outside roots/frontiers, every old F/payload root, and the new C/K empty clauses.
All remaining NP duties are outside C and were present in the old complete conjunction.
Take their exact old required children and outside endpoint/middle choices, reissue contexts and apply the same first-birth rule to newly born C rows.
Protected base paths retain the exact ancestor/correspondence contracts.

Induct on old W subtree height for every selected child.
C own-retirement children are omitted because those extensions are now primitive; no visited NP judgment justifies itself.
Same-context component recurrence closes only the component conjunction, not the existential proof induction.
The result is a finite complete forest retaining all inherited fresh roots and independent Gamma duties, without cross-context receipt transfer.

### Native proof-to-control map

The [recursive-copy control](../test/research/re01/private-observed-copy-erasure.test.ts) reuses its existing [source fixture](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/recovery/private-observed-copy-erasure.xsd).

The new control instead uses exact original q as D's middle, making five fresh E rows eligible while retaining every old helper and scaffold.

Joint reconstruction empties the five eligible rows and rebases them to K, yielding one evidence node and 52 members.
All 26 actual-Gamma records and all 25 old fresh roots plus K are checked; every old member, declaration, particle, owner and all 20 noncomplex fresh scaffolds remain.

The replacement has 51 current-H component receipts: original R remains available but is no longer H-consumed, while its separate Gamma receipt remains present.
The test preserves exact outside D/q selectors and order keys, checks all five rebuilt types are primitive, and requires no remaining NP or construction obligations.
It explicitly permits changed C-to-original property comparisons because their C-owner queries have disappeared.
This executed construction supports the theorem's discriminator; it does not establish a general input-derived inventory bound by experiment.

### Remaining acceptance gates

The transformation covers a supplied finite forest and a stated eligible family.
It does not bound retained nominal/member paths, anchored non-NP copies, semantic/scalar ports, helper multiplicity or encoded fields.
Whole-history marks may retain a birth-E identity that becomes anchored only after retirement; a later equality does not bound its earlier birth height.

A persistent activated query cannot first mark a fresh current own-NP operand through a distinct property pair: its already marked, distinct owner survives that operand's mandatory E-to-R retirement, which makes the forced pair fail.
This observation requires that persistence premise; marking alone does not make an original owner's current-H query persist in the child.
This pointwise restriction does not remove the history-late R exception or supply a copy count.
Original/global/source seed roles and current base paths still need a joint finite inventory.

General successful-construction coverage, universal executable evidence adequacy, complete negatives, mathematical termination and dated correspondence remain open.
The scalar support-word realization and finite nominal/global/helper inventory must coexist with this reconstruction before a general decision procedure can rely on them.
B-v1, all seven recovery contracts, P1/F2, original contexts and #179/#153/#184 gates remain unchanged.

### Assessed fixed values at the particle boundary

This investigation exposed a concrete caller defect independent of the helper bound.
The dated Structures element value-property mapping uses the declared simple type or simple content type, and otherwise builtin string; NameAndTypeOK compares those assessed values.
The declaration owner already supplies that assessment, including its exact declared-TYPE dependencies and mixed-emptiable prerequisite.

The relation's component-validity and particle-local-legality prerequisites remain unchanged.
Full consumed declaration construction still checks its exact active TYPE, formal mixed emptiability and scalar formation in that context; a string fallback receipt proves none of those duties by itself.
No Gamma receipt moves into H.

The [independent particle controls](../test/unit/particle-restriction-independent.test.ts) add eleven cases using a [qualified source fixture](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/recovery/mixed-fixed-particle-independent.xsd).
Equal/unequal mixed and anyType values, integer aliases, missing fixed fields, both current-H complex dependencies and pending raw simple-content formation distinguish the repair.
A complete zero-node primitive forest checks all 47 actual records, including a genuine restriction with two distinct local declarations.
A legal nonempty mixed TYPE still makes its fixed declaration fail full construction even though the local value comparison is conditionally true.
This preserves the distinction between local comparison and complete legality; it supplies no general negative or evidence-bound theorem.

## Common mixed payload without changing the nominal fresh family

### Conditional statement

Fix a qualified actual schema Γ, an exact retained parent P, and a supplied finite complete founded forest W.
Let F be a role-closed family of genuinely post-P complex identities, present with complete raw rows wherever used in W.
Every F content is nonscalar: empty, element-only or mixed; its immediate base is in F or builtin anyType, denoted UR.
In every retained context, every complex base consumer entering F also belongs to F.
This includes endpoint versions and their selected child intermediates, rather than only the initial parent graph.

Every surviving positive production property query whose owner lies outside F is closed at the F boundary.
A forced distinct complex pair entering F has both operands in F; an exact identity pair remains an identity shortcut.
Queries owned inside F are reconstructed from the common recipe instead of preserving their old payload tasks.
These are hypotheses about W's genuine query owners and forced pair closure, not a test of arbitrary standalone comparisons.

No original/P payload, original declaration TYPE, scalar facade, typed facet, genuine scalar recipe or foreign middle fixes an F payload.
Independently required simple-content components are excluded; unwrapping an operand does not erase their full-component duty.
Original Γ operands, source bindings, namespace contexts and protected outside scalar/source fields remain exact.
An F record used only as immutable lexical-owner metadata retains its exact identity and context.

Then F can select one common mixed payload before birth while preserving a complete forest with the same NP child geometry.
The construction does not find F, bound its size or establish that every qualified input has such W.

Reserve the whole finite retained W, including later births and all original/source/private identity reservations.

Reissue each native view in its exact rebuilt H; collision freedom and authority are checked, not inferred from hash uniqueness.
The allocation premise includes the private UR view identities generated for every retained family member.

Choose w = ANY ##any/lax at 0..unbounded, q = SEQ1[w], and p = SEQ1[q].
The ordinary wildcard w receives neither a native ur-origin marker nor an original-content handle.
Select mixed p, no effective AUs, and a universal lax attribute wildcard with an empty sources list for every F birth.
Canonical mixed/content and complete raw fields agree; canonical derivation contentKind remains complex.
Keep each F identity, QName, owner/path, context, method, immediate base, flags and existing evidence choices.

The new nonderivation recipe is chosen at first birth and retained exactly in every later endpoint version.
This is not a later change to an inherited nonendpoint payload.
Every old real payload particle, declaration, AU and scaffold remains a member and mandatory fresh root.
An owner's newly selected content need not select every real particle that it still owns.
Outside canonical/raw roots, real identities, TYPE selectors, coherent order keys and all Γ/P records remain unchanged.

### Existing owner proof map

The shared p is legal, UPA-valid, EDC-valid and schema-emptiable, with one wildcard incidence after restriction normalization.

No property read transfers Γ validity or imports full validity of an unrelated backing type into H.

### Nominal, property and value observations

Type relations keep identity, namedness, method, base and final/block data.
Thus declared type paths, affiliations, effective membership censuses and EDC type identities remain exact jointly.

The exact method/base graph preserves each own-NP subject and designated ancestor.

The new test is free-ur-common-mixed-payload.test.ts, SHA256 790195e3c99d7f2a9cf86eb756dafd08f8446bd78c93be40d6aec415a25a71e6.
Its fixture SHA256 is 3ea86ad48d91dbfc61b5a2cab7aef31619f8695476bbbeb36e48d13bd4c27067.
Author qualification passed 69 distinct tests in seven files, RE01 strict, toolchain and diff checks.
No reference-engine forest oracle, general SC extension or universal native materialization theorem is asserted.
Family extraction, general coverage, input-derived counts, complete negatives, termination and dated correspondence remain open.

## Pure input and output contract

The proposed production input is immutable accepted analysis plus a specific extension type and its original ancestor chain. It retains original particles/group uses, local and group-use bounds, original attribute uses/declarations, wildcard namespace/process contexts, scalar and element fixed/default operands, `final`/`block`, content mode and all source/QName/chameleon context. An effective interval or flattened attribute object is insufficient.

The research [probe](../test/research/re01/witness-probe.ts) accepts small prepared `View` records with original source IDs, exact decimal occurrence strings and atomic original type references. A certified preparation stage is an explicit precondition; it performs no schema loading, normalization, occurrence arithmetic, datatype interpretation or payload matching. The pair predicate is supplied separately and charges the same budget.

| Result | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `particle-witness` | Constructive particle candidate under stated preparation/predicate preconditions |
| `unresolved` | Missing predicate authority, uncertified normalization or failed finite family without a full negative theorem |
| `resource-limit` | Request cannot finish within budget; no candidate or partial mapping survives |
| Production `invalid-schema` | Requires a proved rule violation, such as the unrelated original attribute type contrast |

Production #179 must check the complete intermediate and final restriction before emitting a type-level witness. A particle result alone cannot mark an operation supported. Rule interpretation, witness proof, production implementation support and payload validity remain separate claims.

## Parameterized sibling predicates

### AU01

[AU01](content-model-s06-au-01.md) supplies the original deduplicated AU-ID set, declaration identities, requiredness, effective value constraints and source provenance. Extension preserves all original AUs and unions local sets; QName/declaration uniqueness is checked before restriction. It does not replace inherited uses with a scalar keyed by QName.

For a single unambiguous base use, restriction preserves requiredness, checks valid simple-type derivation and retains applicable fixed constraints. The selected multi-use contract checks each actual source replacement against every matching original base use; omission retains the original use set unchanged. It does not cross-check every retained inherited fixed use against every other retained use.

Adding an intermediate `##any/skip` attribute wildcard appears sufficient for final QNames absent from the preserved base AU set. A complete finite AU witness theorem must additionally specify hypothetical source incidence and final component equivalence, as detailed below. A wildcard cannot bypass the selected checks for an actual replacement of a matching QName.

### PW01

[PW01](content-model-s06-pw-01.md) supplies normalized particle-to-wildcard restriction, namespace subset/compatibility and process strength, with exact formal total range and original wildcard declaration contexts. The universal `0..unbounded` member is unaffected by the alternative member-minimum interpretations: every nonnegative local bound is contained, with unbounded maximum preserved.

Pass formal ranges of the actual operand at the point where the restriction rule invokes them. Do not blindly reuse a pre-normalization group's total range after that group collapsed to an element or wildcard; conversely, do not apply pointless schema normalization to payload languages.

### DT01 and scalar relations

[DT01](content-model-s06-dt-01.md) supplies exact typed value equality and a separate ordered comparison returning less, equal, greater or indeterminate under the selected calendar contract. Original type, lexical value, QName context, list items, declared union-member order and facet layers accompany each operand. An unavailable or exhausted scalar implementation, or legitimate indeterminate ordering, must never become a false equality/type-restriction result.

The initial fixtures use string/token/int without calendar operands. Full-domain RE01 proofs depend on the reviewed selected scalar predicates where original element or attribute fixed/default constraints invoke them. No local Date-based or approximate scalar engine is introduced.

## Follow-up: finite AU synthesis and witness identity

The selected source-replacement contract gives a finite conditional AU synthesis direction. It also exposes a specific missing RE01 theorem about the final hypothetical type's identity and representation. This gap belongs to reordered witnesses, rather than reopening AU01's selected predicate for real source declarations.

### Why the minimal intermediate is insufficient

Let `A` contain an optional AU to global declaration `g:xs:int`, with use-fixed value `1` and no declaration-fixed constraint. Let the final type retain that original AU and add a second optional AU to the same global declaration, use-fixed `2`. The selected C1 rule admits those individually valid schema components and preserves both predicates, even though no instance satisfies them.

If a hypothetical intermediate contains only the original AU plus an attribute wildcard, declaring both final uses as source replacements forces fixed `2` to be checked against the original fixed `1`, so the replacement fails. Instead, an intermediate can extend with the second use and a vacuous final restriction can inherit both unchanged. Thus source replacement and source omission must remain distinct during witness synthesis.

[The original-source fixture](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/re01/au-source-original.xsd) combines that AU contrast with removal/restoration of optional particle `a`, restored as integer. [The hypothetical-source fixture](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/re01/au-source-witness.xsd) supplies the required empty-choice/wildcard intermediate and puts fixed `2` in the intermediate extension, while the final restriction omits attribute `g`. Both historical reference engines rejected both schemas; those observations do not decide the selected C1 or RE equality question.

The final AU predicates and global declaration reference have the same values and incidence pattern in the two sources. The new direct AU occurs under original `D` in one source and intermediate `E` in the other, however, so these are different newly mapped AU components. The committed reference check explicitly verifies the changed source contribution and does not label the new component as the original inherited ID.

### Shared finite AU set criterion

Partition original ancestor and final AU sets by expanded QName, preserving distinct AU identities, declaration identity, original scalar references and original typed constraints. Every intermediate retains the ancestor's original AU set. The displayed set criterion is shared by both representation proposals; the following source-construction discussion assumes `D2`'s final equality and representation obligations are specified and proved, rather than imposing those source obligations on `D1`.

For an ancestor QName whose original AU set is contained in the intended final set, add the extra final uses in the first extension and omit that QName in the final restriction. For any other ancestor QName, keep only its original uses in the intermediate and construct the final set as source replacement; each replacement must pass every original matching base-use predicate. An empty final set instead needs a legal prohibition of every original optional use; an original required use makes that branch impossible.

For QNames absent from the ancestor, a universal intermediate attribute wildcard admits the final source declarations. The wildcard's `##any/skip` namespace/process combination is an expressible XSD 1.0 extension union and supports every legal final wildcard restriction. It adds no second ID-derived declaration to the intermediate.

Necessity of the replacement/prohibition branches follows from preservation: arbitrary additional intermediate uses cannot remove an original base use. Omission can only inherit a final set containing all originals; actual source replacement must check every original match under the selected contract. Additional uses cannot rescue a replacement that fails an original predicate.

The remaining XML-source sufficiency obligation is not a search bound. It is to prove that adding the final extra uses in the intermediate and inheriting them produces the same permissible final type definition, with the required AU identity/sharing and declaration-scope incidence, under the proposed XML-domain equality rule. Direct attribute syntax creates fresh AU components; group references can reuse existing members but cannot automatically reference an arbitrary direct use owned by `D`.

### Primary evidence about component identity

[Components and Properties](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#scIntro) models a schema as a labeled directed graph and states:

> Equality of components for the purposes of this specification is always defined as equality of names (including target namespaces) within symbol spaces.

The added [component-identity note](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#no-identity) under Type Derivation OK expressly qualifies the identity relation:

> The wording of clause 2.1 above appeals to a notion of component identity which is only incompletely defined by this version of this specification.

It identifies named top-level components, necessarily identical types and identity by construction, including inherited attribute declarations, as settled cases. It then states:

> In other cases two conforming implementations may disagree as to whether components are identical.

[Attribute Use properties](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#AU_details) are requiredness, attribute declaration and value constraint. They contain no intrinsic original-source owner or source-replacement flag; those arise from representation mapping. [Attribute declaration scope](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#Attribute_Declaration_details) does carry a global or owning-complex-type scope, with separate treatment for group declarations.

[Schema Information](https://www.w3.org/TR/2004/REC-xmlschema-1-20041028/#sic-schema) defines an information item isomorphic to a component for PSVI contributions. It supplies useful graph vocabulary, but does not by itself adopt isomorphic unnamed components as identical reordered witnesses. The abstract-model description likewise does not automatically discharge source-representability.

### Concrete equality options

| RE witness equality option | Benefit | Missing obligation or compatibility effect |
|---|---|---|
| Literal original component identities | Strong operand retention | Arbitrary direct AU sharing may lack an XML source representation |
| Abstract component sharing without source reconstruction | Finite AU set construction | Must justify this as the numbered rule's intended witness domain |
| Source-representable final type with incidence-preserving bijection | Represents fresh intermediate AUs honestly | Must define permitted renaming and preservation of local scopes and scalar identities |
| Property-only AU or QName equality | Simple comparison | Loses duplicate-use sharing and can erase original constraints |

An XML-source witness would need an explicit property-and-incidence-preserving bijection for hypothetical artifacts, with all real original operands immutable and provenance retained. It must fix named/global declaration references, distinguish distinct versus shared AU members, preserve requiredness and original typed fixed/default constraints, respect local declaration scopes and anonymous scalar derivation identity, and identify which hypothetical source determines replacement versus omission. This is a conditional proposal rather than an accepted replacement for literal original identities; the continuation below also examines an abstract-component domain.

That proposal must specify the exact comparison closure. A bijection over the final type's non-derivation component graph need not preserve the same cross-root sharing as a bijection that also anchors every original ancestor AU and every named attribute-group member. Omitting the original derivation edge is necessary for reordering, but does not by itself decide which other component identities remain anchored.

Source construction also cannot select an arbitrary subset of an existing group's AU members by referencing that group. A group reference imports its complete member set; extra members can introduce a declaration QName conflict with a use that every extension must retain from the ancestor. Copying just the desired member into direct syntax creates fresh AU/declaration components instead, so the sufficiency proof must cover complete group imports, conflicts, local scopes and any permitted bijection rather than treating group-owned uses as freely selectable references.

The original real `D` source and its AU plan never change. A hypothetical restriction uses its own declared local/group contributions to classify replacement; it cannot reuse the real original `D` contribution flags while pretending freshly constructed intermediate uses were already inherited. A witness-to-original correspondence would retain original source operands as diagnostic/provenance targets instead of rewriting their IDs or context.

### Executable named-group comparison contrast

The continuation starts from the exact unmerged [PR #254 candidate](https://github.com/TechSpokes/typescript-wsdl-client/pull/254), commit `ddd90663aa88670edcca4371f7a142d94a5d36da`, tree `f00f442b4306285c20e0f6524ae50c652e0e50b6`. Its accepted NT-CONT-01 evidence contract supplies one live Node primary, selected independent checks, pinned historical observations and explicit unqualified capabilities. None of those evidence kinds establishes RE01 completeness.

The new [original group source](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/re01/au-group-original.xsd) declares global `g:xs:int` and `h:xs:int`. Named group `G` contains a use of global `g` fixed to `2` and a use of global `h`; ancestor `A` instead has global `g` fixed to `1` and a distinct qualified local declaration `h:xs:int`.

Original restriction `B` prohibits optional local `A/h`, retaining `A/g`. Final extension `D` imports `G`, so its final AU set contains original `A/g`, `G/g` and `G/h`. The selected C1 rule retains both fixed `g` predicates; their empty accepted-instance set does not itself establish schema invalidity.

The [hypothetical source](../test/conformance/fixtures/xsd/re01/au-group-witness.xsd) keeps original `A`, `G` and both global declarations unchanged. Its intermediate `E` adds a fresh direct global `g` use fixed to `2`, while retaining original `A/g` and local `A/h`. A fresh one-member group `H` references global `h`; hypothetical `D` restricts `E` with `H` and omits `g`.

That final `H/h` contribution is an actual source replacement. It passes the requiredness, named scalar derivation and fixed-value predicates for the optional unconstrained original `A/h`; omitted `g` inherits original `A/g` and fresh `E/g` without turning them into replacements. Both final graphs refer to the same global declarations and named `xs:int`, avoiding an anonymous scalar or local final-scope ambiguity in this contrast.

The supplied final AU correspondence maps `A/g` to itself, `G/g` to fresh `E/g`, and `G/h` to fresh `H/h`. It preserves distinct versus shared nodes, declaration references, requiredness and exact fixed predicates.
